# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0002 — SECOND REVIEW REPAIR PASS: WHO OWNS *ABOUT TWO HUNDRED ROOMS*

**This block wins over everything above it. Its record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0002.md` §11. A repair pass moves no age, no descriptor, no trade, no second handle, and invents and removes nobody, and nothing in this block does.**

**THE FINDING, AND IT IS A CAST FILE FINDING AND THAT IS WHY IT IS HERE.** **A private figure had been lent to three men across the ten chapters of Batch 0002 and it belongs to two of them, and a cast file is where a later writer runs the map. A third holder stands outside this batch and is named below.** The second review repair pass found four paragraphs in Chapters 0368, 0369 and 0370 giving *about two hundred rooms* — and in one of them a second, larger borrowing with it — to a man of about thirty-nine, twice to a man of about thirty-one with a predicate that is not his, and once to nobody in particular in a paragraph whose only candidate was a man who had never been in a room in this city and who is the subject of the sentence. **All four are on their own pages now.**

**1. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING — the figure is his, and it was on a page in Volume 07 before this volume began.** *In about two hundred rooms and about eleven kitchens a month, and the only person in nine kitchens nobody asked anything in* — this block's entry above, `state/open-threads.md` item 9, and `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0343.md:29` in a different form: *a man who carries things for a living has been in about two hundred rooms in this city and has never once improved on a thing anybody said to him*. **He also, on a page in this batch, `chapter-0361.md:49`, has not asked one question in four times up this road — and that is the not-asking of him that Chapters 0369 and 0370 now use, and no longer about rooms.** His second handle is unchanged and no chapter of this pass touched it.

**2. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT — the same figure, in his own mouth, and it is his.** *I have been in about two hundred rooms in this city and I have not been asked anything in about nine of them, and the other hundred and ninety are rooms somebody else sent me to, and I have heard everything that has ever been said in front of me and I have never once repeated any of it. That is a trade and not a favour* — `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0367.md:35`, and the narration at `:37` and `:61` and `chapter-0352.md:21`. **Chapter 0368 had lent both halves of that to the man of about thirty-nine, and the man of about thirty-nine has no room count and no such trade on any page, and what is on his page now is that he has carried a figure round four markets for four years and has never once said one out loud where anybody was standing.** His second handle is that nib and it is untouched. **AND A THIRD HOLDER EXISTS AND IS NOT IN THIS BATCH'S TEN CHAPTERS, AND THE FIGURE IS THEREFORE ON THREE MEN IN THIS VOLUME AND NOT ON TWO: `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0360.md:45` gives it to Adrian Vale, in *the shortest true thing he has said in about two hundred rooms in this city*, which is Batch 0001's file, which this pass did not edit, and which no review of this batch raised. It is named here so that the two above do not read as a complete list, and it is carried in the same debt. Three men, one figure, and the reason two of them were fixed and the third was not is scope and not judgement.**

**3. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE HAS NO PRIVATE FIGURE OF HIS OWN AND NOW HAS NONE.** He is the man who said a number out loud in Chapter 0369 and was thanked by nobody, and the man who put a board of nineteen rates down flat on a wet board in Chapter 0368, which nobody in this city has ever seen him do. **That is the whole of what is on his page about how many rooms he has been in, and the answer is that the record never said and a paragraph did.**

**AND THE DEBT, WHICH IS THE POINT OF THE BLOCK AND IS NOT PAID.** **Two men carry about two hundred rooms and both carry a nine in a second place: nine kitchens nobody asked anything in, and nine of two hundred rooms he has not been asked anything in.** **Both figures are in their own mouths or in a state file quoting a mouth, neither was put there by this batch, this pass changed neither number, and a batch may not decide that two men are not the same figure.** It is carried at `state/open-threads.md` for the Volume 08 close, **which is the only phase after an outline phase permitted to decide anything.** A later writer who needs to tell these two men apart should use the second handle, and each of them has one, and the two handles are the sheet of chalk and the folding knife.

**AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE IN ANY PERSON.** No age, no descriptor, no trade and no second handle moved. No person was invented and none was removed. The man of about fifty-nine is still nobody's man and is still the last image's man and is still in two chapters of this batch and no other. The woman of about fifty-two is still the refusal of the fourth place and is still not the woman of Chapter 0266. Adrian Vale is still in no chapter of this batch in any form, and the paper in the inside of his coat is still his, and nobody in these ten chapters opened it.

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0003 — the state of every person these ten days put on a page

**This block wins over everything above it. A batch moves no age, no descriptor, no trade and no second handle, invents and removes nobody, and nothing in it does. Its record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0003.md`. Where this file and a chapter disagree about a day, a name or a figure, the chapter is right.**

**1. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER — she wrote the sixth notice of the volume and she was not in the room where the question was asked.** Chapter 0371 she writes a sheet with a day and a place on it and nothing at the top of it, rules it into two by hand, and says out loud that the reason there is no name is that if you come to that room on that day then afterward there is nothing whatever in four hundred miles that can say you were there. Chapter 0372 she has her book open on the table and asks him nothing. Chapter 0374 the room decides out loud not to say what it is for and she is not in that room. **Chapter 0375 SHE IS NOT IN THE ROOM. The question was asked at about the eighth hour and she came up to the step at the back with no name on it at about the tenth hour with the slate under her arm and did not come in and stood there until about the fourth hour of the afternoon with the door open behind her, and the two women of about twenty-nine were about four paces apart for as long as she was there and said nothing to each other, and the arrangement on the page in Chapter 0358 held without anybody touching it.** Chapter 0378 she is in the room and says nothing while two documents are counted aloud in it. Chapter 0379 she says out loud that she has not looked at nine rows at the back of her own book since the eighth day of that month, which is fifty-six days back from the sixty-fourth, that she cannot tell what day any of it happened on, and the reason nine of those rows are not going to be copied. Her second handle is her second slate and it is on a shelf with a date on it and nothing under it in five of these ten chapters and nobody in four hundred miles picked it up. *Open.*

**2. TAMSIN QUILL — she asked one question, once, and she has not given the sheet to anybody.** Chapter 0373 she unfolds the sheet on her knees on the bottom step of nine steps in the open, folds it again in four with the same four creases, says out loud that the fourth crease has gone soft and that that is the whole of what has changed on it, and gives her reason for saying it out loud. She does not hand it to a man standing about nine feet off who has told her he counted the places on it and did not ask. **Chapter 0375 SHE ASKED THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHETHER IT WILL GO, two words, once, in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, in front of about nine people, and she did not ask it twice and she did not stand up.** Chapter 0380 she puts the sheet on a bench in the middle of a room in front of about nine people and does not hand it round, and says out loud that the about nine places on it and the about nine people in the room are two nines and not the same nine and that she is saying so before any of them works it out, and then that the sheet is not going to anybody, not to be written out, not into a form, and not to a man who fills in four places four times a year. **The consent fracture is not mended in any of these ten chapters, nobody apologises, the fifth condition is not given, and the fracture is visible in these ten only as a bench with a man at one end of a room and a woman at the other who have not said one word to each other about it.** Her second handle is the sheet and it is the same in every chapter she is in. *Open.*

**3. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-ONE — she was asked whether it will go and the answer is not on a page.** She is at a bench with the pen in her apron and not in her hand at the seventh hour or the eighth, in Chapter 0375; she is at a bench with her hands in her apron in Chapter 0372 and does not take the pen out. **Nobody asked her anything except that once, nobody asked her a second time afterwards, nobody in that room is going to be the second one, and she has not been made to start waiting to be told what she is for again.** Her second handle is the nib the man of about forty-eight ground on a step outside a room, invented on the page in Chapter 0367 and named out loud by him once, and **no chapter of this batch says it and no chapter improves on the two words she said about it in Chapter 0367.** *Open, and the question she was asked is not answered anywhere in this batch's ten chapters and is not in any file this batch produced.*

**4. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT — he said out loud, for the first time in nine years, that he has never filled in one of four places.** Chapter 0372, at the near end of a bench, about nine feet from the table, with his hands on his knees and no slate out. **He has filled in the first, second and third of the four places four times a year since the winter before the water out of what he is given, and he has never once filled in the fourth one, and nobody has ever sent him anywhere to find out how, and he has assumed since the winter before the water that it is a share of something and that a share of something has to come out of a household, and he has never gone to a household.** He said he is not going to and is not going to be asked, and that if he is asked he will say the same three sentences in the same order. **He was asked nothing in that room and about nine people in it declined to, and he went back down nine steps to the wharf and did not take a tally-board out and put a figure on it before he went.** He is in no other chapter of these ten. His second handles are a tally-board, a knife and a barrow and none of them was used in Chapter 0372. **The figure he has in his head, as against the figure he sends, is on no page and is printed in none of these ten chapters and in no file this batch produced.** *Open, and the two acts — the answer of day 357 and the refusal of day 393 — are not in one room on one day and he has not been asked anything since the forty-second day of that month and has not wanted to be.*

**5. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — he is the person who keeps two things apart out loud, and he said no out loud about a bench, and his chalk is still in the pocket.** Chapter 0371 he sat through a sheet being written and sent and did not offer it. Chapter 0372 he said to a table, without turning round, that a man had not filled the last place in, which is a statement and not a question. Chapter 0373 he counted about nine places on a woman's page from about nine feet off and told her he had counted it and had not asked her one thing. **Chapter 0378 HE PUT A HAND OUT OVER A BENCH TOWARDS A DOCUMENT AND STOPPED, and said out loud that he was not going to put the two four-place forms together and that he had been trying since a Tuesday on a lane and could not do it then and can see that he was wrong to try, and then counted eight places aloud and said that the third place in the case is a word nobody in this city has said out loud in a room in about nine years and that he is not going to say it and that that is not modesty.** He is in Chapters 0371, 0372, 0373, 0374, 0375, 0377, 0378, 0379 and 0380 of this batch. **His piece of chalk has not been out of his own inside breast pocket since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day 298, and in Chapter 0377 he had both hands on a pole in a squall and could not get at it and did not try.** He is not thanked for anything in these ten and did not ask. *Open.*

**6. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE — he is at the back of seven of these ten rooms and says nothing in six of them and is not asked one question in any.** Chapters 0371, 0372, 0374, 0375, 0378, 0379 and 0380, in every one of them against a wall with his own slate flat on his knee. In Chapter 0378 a room counts eight places across two documents about nine feet from where he is standing and nobody in that room asks him whether it is right. In Chapter 0379 he sits about nine feet from nine rows nobody can date and says nothing. **He has no room count on any page and none was given to him in these ten and none may be; he has a board, nineteen rates, four markets, four years, and a name he took off that board in his own hand about nine years back and has never said out loud to anybody, and he did not say it in any of these ten chapters.** His second handle is that board and his own slate. *Open, and he is right in Chapter 0393 and is not this batch's.*

**7. ADRIAN VALE — he is in two of these ten chapters and in no other of them, in any form.** **Chapter 0371, a room, reading a page: a sheet folded into four with a date on the back and two names on the front of it, taken out of the inside of his own coat and put flat on a table about nine feet from a sheet that had just gone out with nothing at the top of it, read from the top down, not turned over, put back, and the coat buttoned. Neither of those two facts was said out loud by anybody in that room and nobody asked him what the paper was.** **Chapter 0376, a yard behind a row of kitchens at the end of a lane, with a woman of about sixty-nine in it and an empty tin she did not open; he watched about five hours, worked out the order nine things are done in, said it out loud in order, was told he had one of the nine the other way round, was not told which one, and went away along a lane without being told whether he was right.** The man of about fifty-two who digs is not in that yard and nobody in it is going to ask him anything. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world was not named once, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, and **the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files and he is aged nowhere. THE FIGURE *ABOUT TWO HUNDRED ROOMS* IS NOT USED IN EITHER OF HIS TWO CHAPTERS AND THE VOLUME'S OWN RULE FORBIDS IT IN BOTH.** *Open, and `outline/volume-08.md` §4 names him in 0352, 0355, 0360, 0371, 0376, 0381 and 0392 and in no other chapter of the volume.*

**8. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING — he carried the sixth notice down nine steps and said out loud, in a room, who was not going to be told what it was for.** Chapter 0371: he took the folded sheet off a table, did not look at the face of it, and said that he has not opened one of these he has carried and that the reason is not carefulness but that a man who has opened them has read days of a city he was not asked about, and that he got that by carrying things and not by being asked for anything. **Chapter 0374 he stood at the end of the bench and said that nobody in this city is going to be told what that room is for, and that includes the four people who are going to come up those steps, and that includes the woman sitting at the far end of the bench with a pen she has not taken out of her apron, and then said the figure that is his own: about two hundred rooms in this city, and that he has never once improved on a thing anybody said in one of them and has never once been the man who asked what a room was for.** Chapter 0378 he was at the end of the bench and counted eight places along with the man who said them. Chapter 0377 he tied a line on the far corner of a market roof that nobody had asked him to. **His figure is in this batch once and it is in his own mouth and it is his, and it is not in any other chapter of the ten, and no figure of his was changed.** *Open.*

**9. THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT — he is in two of these ten chapters and the second is the midpoint.** He sat down the moment he came in, the way a man does who has learned that standing about in a room is what a person does when they are expecting to be asked. **Chapter 0375 he looked at the second slate on the shelf for a while and then said two things: that it has been on that shelf for about a month with a day on it and nothing under it and that nobody in that room has picked it up, and then the figure that is his own — about two hundred rooms in this city, and he has not been asked anything in about nine of them. About four of the nine in that room had known that figure for about a fortnight and not one of them had said it in that room before.** He has a folding knife with a hinge that does not spring and he opened it and shut it with his thumb. Chapter 0376 he was at the second kettle in the yard. **The figure is in this batch once and it is in his own mouth and it is his and it is not in any other chapter of the ten and no figure of his was changed.** *Open, and see the debt at item 9 of the block above.*

**10. THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE — his nine words are still in the inside of his coat.** He came up the nine steps with them in there in Chapter 0375, with his hands in the pockets of his coat, and he said nothing while a woman said out loud that she and another woman had been arranging the same bench for a month without speaking, and about four people in that room worked out that he has a page of his own that nobody in this city can ask to have produced and that the only person who could have said something about two women not speaking is a man who is not in the room and is not going to be sent for. **He has not taken them out in front of anybody in these ten and he is not going to.** *Open.*

**11. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE — her tin was in her hands the whole time and she did not open it.** Chapter 0376, a yard behind a kitchen at the end of a lane, with about nine lines across it and a wall down one side. She told a man she had spoken four words to in four hours that he had the second kettle the other way round and that she was not going to tell him which one he had wrong, and neither was anybody else in that yard, **and she gave the reason: a man who is told has it right tomorrow and a man who is not told will have it wrong tomorrow and will think about it, and this yard has nine things in it and about nine of us have found that out standing here.** She went back inside about a quarter of an hour after he had gone. **Her second handle is the tin and it is empty and it did not open, and the folded sheet thirty years old that goes in it was not in that yard, is not in any chapter of this batch, and nobody may un-condition it.** *Open.*

**12. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR AT THE STALL — a number went back to the woman who owns it.** Chapter 0377, a Sunday, the sixty-second day of the Bare Month, a market under sailcloth and sacking on four poles. A roof came down by hand in about a quarter of an hour and the two ends went first because the two ends are where the weight is. **She said the figure out loud before he got to it, and he stopped, and then he said that he has got it and that she has got it and that that is the whole of what has happened on those boards this morning, and that he is not going to be thanked and is not going to say that he said it first, and that is so that the market does not work it out.** She said it twice more before the light went, both times to nobody in particular. **No paragraph of these ten chapters carries the man of about thirty-four and the woman of about thirty-four together, and the check that would have said otherwise is the unbounded one, because *woman of about thirty-four* contains the string *man of about thirty-four*, which is the false positive `outline/volume-08.md` §20.9 already names.** *Open, and nothing in these ten refers to the day her voice went and nothing may.*

**13. THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WITH THE HANDCART, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE SHUT GATE, THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE'S NINE WORDS, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-ONE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE — none of them is in any of these ten chapters except the man of about twenty-five, and the reason each is absent is not an accident.** The woman of about fifty-two was not asked for her days a second time and is not on a page of this batch. The man of about fifty-seven was not asked where the fence is and was not told. The man of about sixty with the handcart is not in any of these ten and the fence is not in any of these ten. The woman of about fifty at the shut gate was not asked anything; she is quoted once, from what a man says she said to a wall on a lane, and she is not in the room and the brass key on the tarred cord is not in it. The woman of about sixty-one is not in this city in any of these ten chapters and is not going to be asked about anything by anybody and **no chapter of the ten counted the days since she said no at any value**. The man of about fifty-nine is not in any of these ten chapters. *Open.*

**AND WHAT DID NOT MOVE IN ANY PERSON.** No age, no descriptor, no trade and no second handle moved. No person was invented and none was removed. **The volume's own name is spoken once, in Chapter 0357, and is on a page in that chapter and in no file, and this block does not have it and nothing in this block may be copied into a file that does.**

## VOLUME 08, BATCH 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — this block wins over every block above it

**NOT ONE PERSON MOVED.** No age, no descriptor, no trade, no second handle, no second handle removed, no person invented, no person removed, no cast member merged and no cast member split. The prose of Chapters 0371 to 0380 was reworked sentence by sentence and **the set of bolded speech paragraphs in all ten files is byte-identical to the first edition**, so every one of the thirteen numbered items in the block above stands as written. The disposition is `reviews/volume-08/batch-0003.md`.

**AND THE ONE FINDING ABOUT PEOPLE THAT A PROSE PASS CANNOT SETTLE IS RECORDED HERE, BECAUSE IT IS A FINDING ABOUT THE CAST AND NOT ABOUT THE PROSE.** A review found that a reader tracking these ten chapters has to separate the people by the object in each person's hand — **the man of about thirty-four's chalk, the man of about thirty-nine's slate, the man of about forty-eight's folding knife, the man of about thirty-one's satchel with a flap, the woman of about twenty-nine's second slate, Tamsin Quill's folded sheet of road, the woman of about sixty-nine's tin, the man of about twenty-two's nine words** — and that this is a failure of the descriptor map and not a breach of it. **The map is a declared house device at `outline/volume-08.md` §20.8, §17 item 9 requires a second handle beside the age and the trade and never instead of it, and this batch obeyed that instruction in all ten chapters and the obedience was not enough.** It is carried as Debt 7 at `state/open-threads.md` and it is a decision for an outline phase or a close and not for a batch. **The remedy is not to rename anybody and it is not to drop a handle; it is that the two men of about thirty-four and about thirty-nine are the second and third most frequent descriptors in this manuscript and a batch may not invent a third handle for a returning person to solve a problem the map created.**

**AND THE PROTAGONIST'S ABSENCE IS RECORDED HERE BECAUSE IT IS A CHARACTER FINDING AND NOT ONLY A PLANNING ONE.** **Adrian Vale is in two of this batch's ten chapters — 0371 and 0376 — and in five of the volume's thirty, and `outline/volume-08.md` §4 names every chapter of the volume he is in, 0352, 0355, 0360, 0371, 0376, 0381 and 0392, and the batch honoured that list exactly and moved him into nothing.** A review called the thinness blocking and it is: **seven chapters in fifty is thin for a novel whose specification names the man in its first line, and he performs no working, opens no threshold, is aged nowhere, is offered no workway and asks for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* stand at zero across the batch.** **The remedy that moves no day and no beat is a decision for the Volume 08 close and it is Debt 8.** The plan's own contradiction about him at the midpoint is corrected in `outline/volume-08.md` §8, which now defers to §4.

**THE VOLUME'S OWN NAME IS STILL SPOKEN ONCE, IN CHAPTER 0357, AND IS ON A PAGE IN THAT CHAPTER AND IN NO FILE, AND THIS BLOCK DOES NOT HAVE IT AND NOTHING IN IT MAY BE COPIED INTO A FILE THAT DOES.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0004 — the state of every person these ten days put on a page, days 381 to 390, and this block wins over every block above it

**This phase resumed from a checkpoint with the ten chapter files already on disk, and its work on them was a repair pass. No day, no weekday, no month day, no form, no object count, no anchor, no second handle and no descriptor moved, and the age-and-trade form is not abolished. Nobody got stronger in any of these ten chapters and nobody opened a threshold.**

**Adrian Vale — Stage 2 on all ten days, and named in one of them.** In Chapter 0381 he is in the room over the market at the far end of the bench, reading a page that is not his, with a sheet folded into four in the inside of his coat with a date on the back of it and two names on the front of it, and the coat buttoned over it. **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register said out loud in front of about nine people that she was not going to ask him what was in the inside of his coat, that she had known there was a page in it since the first day of that month, and that the not-asking had cost her the same as asking would have.** He performs no working, opens no threshold, is not offered a workway, asks for none, is aged nowhere, and does not name the other world. **He is in no other of the nine chapters in any form, and a card may not move him into one to give him a line, and *about two hundred rooms* is at zero in Chapter 0381 and is forbidden there.** Carried: he is in two chapters of this volume's thirty to date, and that is thin, and the remedy is a decision for the Volume 08 close.

**The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall — a piece of chalk he does not use, and a right hand.** His second handle is unchanged and the chalk has not been out of his own inside breast pocket since the forty-third day of the Short Month, and the full form of that date is printed in six of these ten chapters and is not shortened to a relative phrase in any of them. **He took the weight of a barrow up a wet board on day 382 on his right hand, said nothing about doing it, and could not close that hand again until he put it in the cold water at the bottom of the boards about a quarter of an hour before the light went; he told nobody, and the man of about thirty-eight knew and did not say that he knew.** He stood under a gate from the second hour to the seventh on day 385 as one of thirty-nine men who work for nothing, and has never once said to the person he sells to that the gate costs him a day of selling. **He says the reason out loud before he is asked for it, and he did it five times in these ten days: why a notice is not going to be changed, why he is not going to write on a wall, what a room is going to do to a man who said a number on a road, who is going to go up the nine steps first, and why nobody is going to that household.** On day 384 he said out loud that a share is a figure and a share is a household's. On day 387 he said out loud in a lane, in the open, to a wall, that he has a piece of chalk and is not going to write on it because he has not got anything that is mine to put there, and that it is not modesty and it is not the weather. He is in Chapters 0381, 0382, 0383, 0384, 0385, 0386, 0387, 0388 and 0390 and in no other of the ten.

**The man of about thirty-eight at the wharf — a tally-board, a knife, a barrow.** **He said about four hundred and forty out loud on his own boards on day 383, in his own mouth, having said it at a stall for about a month, and then said, before he was asked, that that one goes on and that there is a second number which has never been on a page in this city and which he is not going to say he believes.** The second figure is printed nowhere and may not be. **He went up the second barrow on the shaft himself on day 382 and would not let anybody help him, and that is not modesty and has not been mistaken for it by anybody in this city.** He has been putting a figure in a column four times a year since the winter before the water, has never once gone to find out what a share is, and said so out loud in a room on day 372. **A sheet with a day and a place and nothing at the top of it went against the wall at the top end of his boards on day 389 with a stone off a doorstep on it, and he looked at it and did not ask what was on it.** He is right about most of what he says. He is not going to be defeated in any of these ten days and he is not defeated.

**The man of about thirty-nine — a board, and his own slate on his knee.** He had been at the back of nine, ten and eleven rooms in a fortnight without being asked one question in any of them, **and then he asked one question, out loud, on a wharf, in the open, where anybody standing on a river path could have heard the answer.** He said out loud on a road on day 385, to thirty-nine men at a gate, that they are three hundred days unpaid measured from the eighty-fifth day of this flood, with both anchors named in the sentence; **it is the first figure anybody in four hundred miles has ever attached to those thirty-nine men, it is on nobody's page, and there is no place in this city for it.** He said out loud in Chapter 0384, the first whole sentence in nine rooms that was not a question, that there are two empty thirds in that room and they are not the same empty. **He said out loud on a wharf on day 389 that he is not going to be sent for the room, because being sent for is being sent for, and after that there is no way for anybody at the far end of anything to find out whether he went or not.** He was told in advance, on day 385, what a room is going to do with what he said on a road, and he did not have to work it out in front of nine people. **His second handle in this batch is the board of nineteen rates, and it is the same board in all three chapters he carries it in and no chapter gives a rate, a price, a market count or a total off it.**

**The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register — her second slate.** The second slate was on the shelf behind the table with a date on it and nothing under it in five of these ten chapters and nobody went near the shelf in any of them, and the third column of her book stayed empty on purpose and nothing was entered in the register in any of these ten. **She said out loud, in front of about nine people, that she was not going to ask a man in her room what was in the inside of his coat, and gave the price of the not-asking.** She read all four places under a heading out loud in the local language on day 384, in daylight, with the day on the door, and the fourth one is a household's. She ruled the seventh sheet by hand on day 388, sent it on day 389, and **said out loud that that is the seventh, that there is not going to be an eighth in this flood, and that a person who is going to write one more ought to say out loud that it is the last one while there is still somebody in the room to hear it.** She has not been up the nine steps since.

**Tamsin Quill — a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded in four, the same four creases, and the fourth crease has gone soft.** **She put it on the bottom step on day 386, in the open, in front of one woman, and was refused, and said out loud that the refusal was of a thing she had already decided and not of a thing she had put in front of the other one, and that the woman she did it to is the one who has to carry the having done it.** She did not take it out of her apron at all on day 390, and said out loud that it is not going in anything they take, not against a wall, not into a book and not on the back of a form, and that the crease that has gone soft is the only part of that page anybody in four hundred miles is losing. **The two women of twenty-nine were never in one room in any of these ten days: the register-keeper is in 0381, 0384 and 0388, Tamsin Quill is in 0386 and 0390, and the sets do not intersect in any file.**

**The woman of about twenty-one — a pen in her apron with the nib ground narrow and cut down with a knife, named out loud by the man of about forty-eight once in Chapter 0367 and not improved on.** **She was asked one question in this flood in a room, on day 375, about whether it will go, and nobody has asked her a second one and nobody ever is going to.** She refused a page standing up on day 386 before the fourth crease was open, and she was not waiting for it, and she said out loud that she has walked nine places in three days and nobody has asked her what is in them and that that is not what is on that sheet. **On day 390 she was asked a different question by a different person — *What will you carry* — and answered *What I am standing in*, without taking the pen out of her apron, and it had nothing in it except a pair of boots and a coat.** She has not started waiting to be told what she is for and nobody is going to ask her.

**The man of about twenty-five — his coat, buttoned, with nine words he wrote himself in the inside of it.** He has not said them in four rooms and he did not say them in Chapter 0387, where he stood nine feet off a wall, put his flat hand on the softest part of the shape on it at about shoulder height, said nothing while his hand was there, and took it off again and put it back in his pocket. He was not asked anything in any of these ten chapters.

**The man of about forty-eight — a folding knife with a hinge that does not spring.** He sat in the room over the market for five hours on day 390, shut and opened the knife about nine times, and said one word. **Nobody in that room found it remarkable and about four of them found the opposite.** He is described in these ten chapters by the second handle and not by the figure: *a man who has been in a great many rooms in this city and has not been asked anything in about nine of them*. **About two hundred rooms is at zero in all ten of these chapters and is not to be used about him in any of them, and the debt of that figure is the Volume 08 close's.**

**The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living — a satchel with a flap.** **He has carried seven of the sheets in this volume to four walls and has not opened one of them, and he said out loud in a room on day 389 that he is not going to say the reason a third time in front of nine people because he has said it twice.** He put the seventh against the wall at the top end of the wharf boards with a stone off a doorstep on it and did not stop. **A man who has carried seven things to four walls without opening one of them is the only person in this city who can be shown to have been in seven places and cannot be shown to have been anywhere.**

**The man of about fifty at the shut gate — a brass key on a tarred cord.** She wrote a date on the post on day 387, did not rub anything out, and said four words out loud to the post, and the four of them were a date and were not for either of the two men. **She has said a date out loud to that post every morning since before the water and has never once said a date to a person, and nobody on that lane is going to ask her about the brass key and nobody has.**

**The man of about sixty with the handcart — a hazel mallet and a coil of rope.** He came up wet market boards on day 382 behind the three barrows, took the mallet out, stood with it in his hand, and did not hit anything. **The cord bound round the head of that mallet is the same cord that is bound round the eleven withies on the fence at the top of a shut road, and Chapter 0382 says in one clause that this is a fact about a piece of cord and is not joined to anything.** He has not used the rope and nobody has asked him about it.

**The man of about fifty-two who digs — a rod he does not carry.** **Not in any of these ten chapters and not asked about by anybody in any of them.**

**The man of about fifty-seven — a length of new rope he has not used.** **Not in any of these ten chapters, not asked where the fence is, not told, and not defeated. He counted it out loud in Chapter 0364 and said *sixteen is right* to the man of about sixty with the handcart, and nobody has asked him anything since. The volume's new question about the fence is asked on day 396 and it is not asked of him.**

**The woman of about sixty-nine — a tin.** **Not in any of these ten chapters.**

**The man of about fifty-nine — a hurdle he made and a bar he replaced.** **Not in any of these ten chapters, nobody's man, and still the last image's man. His years are given as nine years and not in figures, and no chapter of this volume prints a ninth year of this flood.**

**The fourth of the four.** **Not in this city and not going to be asked about anything by anybody, named in those words and in no other words in Chapters 0384, 0386 and 0390. Three of the four said yes in their own words. Nobody counted the days since she said no, at any value, in narration or in a mouth, in any of these ten chapters, and nobody after this volume may ask her about it either unless she asks first.** A party of four is four.

**The woman of about fifty-two whose household's days are asked for — a door she is not asked to open.** **Her refusal is shown standing in Chapter 0384, as the reason the fourth place goes out empty and goes out correct, and it is not reopened, her days are not asked for a second time, and she does not improve on her own sentence.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS: THE STATE OF EVERY PERSON THE SIX REPAIRS TOUCHED — this block wins over every block above it

**Six prose faults and one wrong figure were repaired, and three people in this volume came out of the repair pass with something about them changed, and nothing about anybody else changed at all. No chapter was restarted and no person was added, removed, renamed, re-aged or given a second handle.** The disposition is `reviews/volume-08/batch-0004.md`.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE — WHO HE IS IN THAT ROOM, AND THE COUNT THAT IS HIS AND WAS ON THE WRONG MAN FOR ONE LINE.** He is against the wall at the end of that bench nearest the step, in the band of light where the light stops nine inches past the near end, with his own slate flat on his knee and his right hand over the face of it, and Adrian Vale is at the far end of the same bench nine feet off the table's middle and neither of the two men can be seen from the step at the back. **He is not behind the step and not out of the light in this room, and the phrase *against the wall by the step at the back where the light does not reach* belongs to the other rooms he stands in in this volume and is not to be used of this one.** **The room count is his and it runs: at the back of three rooms in a fortnight on day 363, four on 372 and four on 375, a sixth time in a fortnight on 379, six rooms on 380, eight rooms on 381 — said to him in his own hearing by the man of about thirty-four, who wanted to be able to say out loud that he had worked it out on his own and had been told nothing — nine on 384, ten on 388, eleven on 390, and about nine on 393, the room he refuses to say what the column is for.** He still takes a name off his own board in his own hand, about nine years back, and nine rows at the back of a register are still nobody's to date. He is right about pages and does not turn round at the sound of another person being careful, and that sentence is the man of about thirty-four's and stays his.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — WHO HAS THE COUNT AND WHO HASN'T, AND THE ONE WORD THAT WAS NOT HIS.** **He has no room count on any page in this volume and may not be given one.** What he has is about a fortnight at the near end of that bench without a question in it, and a piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket that has not been taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, in the full form, and a stall he has carried up and down nine steps every morning of this flood, and a right hand that took a barrow up a wet board on day 382 and could not close again for the rest of the day and that he has not mentioned to anybody. **He has chosen a wall over a board, and a board is his second handle, and the sentence he says out loud on the lane is that he has not got anything of his own to put there and that it is not modesty and it is not the weather.** He is the bold voice in Chapter 0382 and he is not going to say whose the two barrows are and never once has and is not going to start on a Friday, and he is the one who offered them, and the man of about thirty-eight is the one who asked, and **the man of about thirty-eight came down to the bottom end of those boards to ask and was going to be answered in his own hearing or not at all.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WHO WORKS THE SALT WHARF — HIS POSITION IN THE EXCHANGE IS NOW ON THE PAGE AND IT WAS NOT.** He came down to the bottom end of the boards for the two barrows that were not his, with his knife in his hand and his tally-board under his arm, and he asked *You are not going to say whose those two are*, and he was told no, and he said *The wheels are dry*, and he was told that a wheel dry since before the water is a wheel nobody has had to use, and that there is exactly one thing in four hundred miles a person can take without anybody being able to say they took it off somebody, and it is a barrow that has not been used. **He is not to be merged with the barrow man, who is not in these ten chapters and may not be put in a room where either of the wharf man's days happens.** He is the man who speaks the name of this volume on day 357 and refuses the wall's rule on day 393, and he is the man who is right about it. He has worked the same boards for nine years, has been in six rooms in a fortnight without being asked a question in any of them, put his knife down flat on the boards for the first time in about nine years, and keeps a second number that is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE — THE WALL AND THE NINE WORDS, UNCHANGED BUT FOR ONE WORD.** He came along the lane at about the eighth hour with his coat buttoned and his hands in the pockets of it, stopped about nine feet off the wall, put his right hand flat on it at about shoulder high on the part of the shape that has gone softest, took it off and put it back in his pocket, and said *You have not got anything to say to it* to a man who had just said out loud that he was not going to write on it. **Nine words he wrote himself are in the inside of that coat and he did not say them in a lane and he is not going to.** The two of them have got a wall between them and neither of them has used it.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE SHUT GATE — THE HOUR, WHICH WAS WRONG AND IS NOW RIGHT.** She came out at about the ninth hour, wrote a date on the post above the day below it in chalk, rubbed nothing out, and said four words out loud to the post, and the four of them were a date and they were not for either of the two men. **The bar goes across at about the tenth hour and not at the seventh, because two of the three people on that lane arrive after the seventh and a gate cannot be barred before the people on its lane have arrived.** She has said a date out loud to that post every morning since before the water and has never once said a date to a person, and nobody on that lane is going to ask her about the brass key and nobody has.

**AND THE FIGURE THAT WAS WRONG IN A FILE AND NOT IN A PERSON.** *About four people in* stands at zero in **five** of the ten and not six, and the six were 0381, 0382, 0383, 0385 and 0386 with 0390 at one. That is a figure about a sentence and not about anybody, and it is published because a record that quietly rewrites itself is the failure this repository names, and **a wrong interpretation sitting in the one item whose job is to certify that this batch watched itself is worse than a wrong count, because a count can be re-run and an interpretation has to be believed.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE, by path: `workspace/volume-08/batch-0005/PROMPT.md`, and it is a batch and not the close, and it now carries three repairs.**

# VOLUME 08, BATCH 0005 — THE AUTHORITATIVE BLOCK, AND IT WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT

**The state of every person this volume carries, after Chapters 0391 to 0400, which are the last ten days of Volume 08. The second handle is beside the age and the trade and never instead of it, and the age-and-trade form is not abolished. The volume's own name is spoken once in Chapter 0357 and is in no file and this block does not have it.**

## THE THREE WHO WENT DOWN A ROAD, AND THE ONE WHO DID NOT

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE — a barrow that is the man of about thirty-eight's, and nine words in the inside of his coat.** He took the wharf man's barrow on the Sunday morning and nobody said why that one. He walked in front of the three of them with his coat buttoned and did not open it at the seventh hour, at the ninth hour, or in between. **The nine words are still in the inside of that coat and he has not said them, and Chapter 0400's last image has him nine days down a road with a barrow and nothing on a page about him.** He said yes in his own words on the sixteenth day of that month, in a room with a date on the door, and he is one of the three of the four.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT — a folding knife with a hinge that does not spring, and a barrow with a nail standing up out of the hub.** He noticed the nail and did not tell the other two and said out loud that he would rather be the man who did not say. **He sat down the moment he stopped, which is a thing he does and has done since before the water, and about four people on that road worked out since that a man who sits down the moment he stops has learned that standing about in front of other people is what a person does when they are expecting to be asked.** He said yes in his own words on the seventeenth day of that month. He is one of the three of the four.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-ONE — a pen with the nib ground narrow and cut down with a knife, in her apron, and a barrow with a coat and a pair of boots in it.** She took the lightest of the three barrows and the whole of what was in it was a coat, a pair of boots and the pen, and that was the whole of what she is carrying. **On the Saturday before, in a room with a date on the door, she was asked a question about luggage and answered *What I am standing in*, and on the Sunday morning somebody asked her whether she had put anything in the barrow and she said she was not going to say it again on a road, and that is the only time in these ten days that the thing came near her mouth, and it did not come out of it.** She said yes in her own words on the fifteenth day of that month. **Her name went at the top of a page that left this city on the seventy-eighth day of that month and the name is on no copy of that page in four hundred miles and is not printed in any file in this repository, and the woman whose name it is is two days down a road she is not on any page for.** Nobody asked her a second question in this volume and nobody is going to.

**THE FOURTH OF THE FOUR.** Said no on day 329, four words, before anybody asked. **Not in this city, not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and named as exactly that in Chapters 0391, 0395 and 0400 and in no other words, and nobody counted the days since at any value in any of the ten chapters or in any of the four state files this batch appended to.**

## THE MEN OF THE ROOM, THE BOARDS AND THE SHUT ROAD

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF — a tally-board, a knife, a barrow, and six words.** He came up the nine steps on the seventy-eighth day of that month because nobody had sent for him, having said out loud on the boards on the Friday before that he was not going to be sent for a room, and he filled in the second of the four places of a return in his own hand, which is about four hundred and forty, and said out loud in the room that there is another one and that it is not going on the form. **The wall told the room that nobody had to be told what a form is for, and he said six words — *I answered, and nobody had to* — and nobody improved on them and nobody thanked him.** On the seventy-ninth day he said out loud on the wharf boards, to nobody who had asked him, that a name at the top of a page is not paid for by the woman whose name it is and not by the woman who wrote it and is paid for by the man who fills in the rest of it correctly and has nowhere to put what he knows; and that in about nine years a man nine hundred miles off is going to write to this city and ask him where the figure came from, and he has it in his head and there is no place on the form for where a figure comes from. **Nobody thanked him and nobody is going to. He is right about the column and the man of about thirty-nine is right about the name and neither of them is going to be argued out of it. He gave a barrow away on a Sunday morning and did not say one word about it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD — a board, and one turn of speech in one room.** **He is in Chapter 0393 and in no other chapter of these ten, in any form, and he is right there, once, and nobody told him he was wrong and nobody told him he was right.** He is against the wall at the end of the bench nearest the step with his own slate flat on his knee. **NO ROOM COUNT IS PRINTED FOR HIM IN ANY OF THE TEN CHAPTERS. The batch prompt said he is at about nine rooms on day 393; the run on the files is four on days 372 and 375, a sixth time on 379, six on 380, eight on 381, nine on 384, ten on 388 and eleven on 390, and `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` prints eleven in a fortnight three days before day 393, and a figure that goes down from eleven to nine over three days is a subtraction across the wrong boundary. The prompt's line is withdrawn at `state/batch-summaries/volume-08-batch-0005.md` §6 item 2. A writer after this one who needs his counter goes to that line and not to a plan.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — a piece of chalk he does not use, and a refusal in six words that is not his.** The chalk is in his own inside breast pocket and has not been out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, in that full form, in Chapters 0392, 0398 and 0400, and is not shortened to a relative phrase in any of the ten. **He said a woman's name out loud in a room in daylight on the seventy-seventh day of that month before anybody asked him for it, and said the price of it out loud before he did it, and nobody wrote it down, and he has not been thanked for it and asked in advance not to be.** In Chapter 0400 he walked past a gate with two bars in it and did not stop and nobody is going to ask him where the two bars are.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER — a book with three columns, and a second slate on a shelf.** She wrote a name at the top of a return on the seventy-seventh day of that month and has not looked at the top of that piece of paper since. **She ruled a line in her book on the eightieth day of that month, put a day in the first column, left the second column empty and left the third column empty on purpose, and entered nothing else at all, and the day in that line is the seventy-sixth day of that month, which is four days back from that morning and not counted forward, and that was said out loud in the room with both days in the sentence.** The second slate was on the shelf behind the table with a date on it and nothing under it on all ten days and nobody went near it. **She is in Chapters 0392, 0393 and 0395 and in no other of the ten, and the other of the two women of about twenty-nine is in Chapter 0392 named as not going to be in that room, and the two of them have not been in one room for about a month and are not going to be.**

**TAMSIN QUILL — a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded in four, the same four creases in the same four places, the fourth of which has gone soft.** **She is in Chapter 0399 and in no other of the ten chapters.** She took the sheet out on the wharf boards on the eighty-fourth day of that month, at about the ninth hour, with nobody near her, opened it along the four creases she has always opened it along, and **about nine places were on it and she did not say any of them out loud and did not point at any of them**, and she folded it the way she folds it and put it back in the front of her apron and put her hand on the front of her apron again. She said out loud that she was not going to give it to anybody and not going to put it on a wall or against one and not going to let it into a book. **She did not look up the road. A man who would have been at the far end of a bench reading a page that was not his is not at the far end of anything and has not been for about a fortnight, and nobody in this city is going to ask her about that and nobody is going to say anything to anybody about it either.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING — a satchel with a flap.** He has carried seven things to four walls in this flood and has not opened one of them. He came along the boards on the eighty-fourth day of that month at about the tenth hour, stopped about nine feet off, looked at the front of an apron and said out loud that he was not going to ask her what she had got in it, and that he had not asked anybody what they were carrying in this flood. **He is not in the room over the market on any of the ten days. The return is still against the wall at the top end of the wharf boards under a stone off a doorstep with a name at the top of it that nobody in this city can produce a copy of.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN — a length of new rope he has never used.** He stood on the low side of a finished fence on the eighty-second day of that month and said out loud that he had wanted to know where it is since before the man beside him started and is not going to be told, and that neither of them is going to be asked and that neither of them is going to settle it this flood. **He was in the room over a market on the eighty-first day of that month for about four hours while a woman asked the room who a fence is for, and he was not asked, and the man of about sixty was in the same room and was not asked, and that is the third time in about a fortnight that anybody in this city has arranged not to ask a man something on purpose. He is right about most of what he says and he is not a villain and he is not leaving and he is not happy, and the new question of this volume went to a room and not to him and is not answered.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WITH THE HANDCART — a hazel mallet with its head bound in cord and a coil of rope.** He put up sixteen willow posts on a shut road and nobody sent him and nobody has asked him and no chapter of this volume asks him. **On the eighty-second day of that month, standing at the top of his own work, he said out loud in a lane that nobody in four hundred miles has ever asked him what it is for, that he had never said, that he was not going to be asked now, that there are people going under it that morning, that he has not been told who and is not going to ask, and that this is the first thing he has ever wanted to know about a thing he made and he is not going to find out.** Nobody asked him and nobody thanked him.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE — a hurdle he made and a bar he replaced, and a barrow.** He walked the four-hundred-mile road four times in nine years and had never once seen anybody on it. **On the eighty-second day of that month he came up a shut road, put his barrow down on the low side at the fence, and there were three people on the road below him, and he said out loud that he is not going to go after them and is not going to ask who they are and that this is the first time he has not wanted to know.** On the eighty-fifth day of that month he came up it again, put the barrow down on the low side at the fence, saw the three on the road, said out loud that nobody is going to ask him either and that he is not going to ask about the fence and is not going to be asked about it, picked his barrow up, went back down the road he had come up, and did not stop at the fence and did not look at it on the way past. **He is the last image's man and he is nobody's man and nobody sent him.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE — a tin, carried a mile, and not left.** She came up nine steps on the eighty-first day of that month with an empty tin in both hands and nobody sent for her. **She asked, to the room and not to anybody: who is a fence for, and then the whole of it — that there are two men in that room who have both stood at it, that neither has ever been asked where it is, that neither is going to be asked now, and she would like that said out loud in a room with a day on the door while there is somebody in it to hear it. Nobody answered it. About four of the nine had an answer and about four of them said nothing, and the answer four of them had was a fence, and nobody said it, and that is the whole of what happened in that room on that day.** The last question she asked in that room was on the thirty-first day of the Bare Month, a Thursday, and that is fifty days back from the morning she asked this one, and it was not answered either. The tin went back into the apron it came in. The sheet thirty years old in her own hand was not in that room and is not on a page in four hundred miles and was not taken out.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT THE SHUT GATE — a brass key on a tarred cord.** She is named in Chapter 0400 as not being asked anything by anybody, and in Chapter 0397 she came out of the shut gate at about the fourth hour, wrote a date on the post, rubbed nothing out, said the date out loud to the post and not to a person, and has never once said a date to a person. **The wall of the shut gate at the end of that lane, with the shape of nine boards gone off it and about four inches of dry stone at the foot of it, is named in Chapters 0397 and 0400 and the boards came off it on the thirty-second day of the Bare Month, a Friday, and there is not a barrow in this city with anything on it and there is not going to be one.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS THE STALL NEAREST THE WATER END — a number she says at the head of her own stall every morning.** She said it once in a squall in the sixty-second day of the Bare Month and nobody asked her for it, and she said it once more on the eighty-third day of that month in a market with its roof back up, and **she said out loud that she is not going to tell the man of about thirty-four what she said and has not written it down and is not going to, and that he has said a number at the head of that stall every morning of this flood and nobody in this city has ever asked him for it either, and that the two of them have got the same arrangement and neither has ever said so out loud.** She looked at the boards and not at him.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS, AND THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHOSE HOUSEHOLD'S DAYS WERE ASKED FOR, ARE IN NONE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.** She said no on a step before anybody asked her, in the forty-first day of the Bare Month, and the fourth place on the return went out empty and went out correct and is not reopened in any of these ten chapters, and her days are not asked for a second time in any of them. **He is eleven miles the other way and is not in any of these ten chapters and was not asked anything about the mark in a field and the mark was not measured, dug, counted or probed.**

**ADRIAN VALE — STAGE 2 ON ALL TEN DAYS, and this is the whole of what changed and nothing changed.** He is in Chapter 0392 and in no other of the ten, in any form, in a room reading the register, which is not his, with the third column of it empty and his finger about a finger's width back from the edge of it and not touching it, and nobody in that room asked him one thing. He performed no working, opened no threshold, named the other world not once, is aged nowhere, was offered no workway and asked for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten files. **He is named in seven chapters of this volume in fifty and Chapter 0392 is the last of them, and the remedy for that is a decision for the Volume 08 close and it may not be made inside a chapter.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE, by path: `workspace/volume-08/close/PROMPT.md`, and it is the Volume 08 close and not a batch and not an outline. Its handoff note is `workspace/volume-08/close/HANDOFF.md` and it decides nothing.**

## THE CAST AS THEY STAND AFTER DAY 400, AT THE VOLUME 08 CLOSE — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**This block is the state of every person Volume 08 put on a page, at day 400, a Tuesday, the eighty-fifth day of the Bare Month. It is a record and not a chapter, and where it and a page disagree the page is right. `state/volume-08-close.md` §1 and §2 and §3 are the long form of this and this block is its head.**

**ADRIAN VALE — STAGE 2 ON ALL FIFTY DAYS, and this is the whole of what changed and nothing changed.** He is named in **seven** of Volume 08's fifty chapters — 0352, 0355, 0360, 0371, 0376, 0381 and 0392 — **and in no chapter in any form in the other forty-three, and 0392 is the last of them.** In six of the seven he is in a room reading a page and 0376 is a yard, and in 0392 he is in a room reading a register that is not his with the third column of it empty and his finger about a finger's width back from the edge of it and nobody in that room asked him one thing. He performed no working, opened no threshold, named the other world not once, is aged nowhere, was offered no workway and asked for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the fifty files. **The rule that produced seven is that he is named in the chapters in which he reads a page and in no chapter in which people decline to ask him, and the finding is that the rule is a rule and not a law. The remedy is a decision and it was taken: Volume 09's plan must include him in at least one chapter in which he reads nothing, and the number is the outline's to set and may not be made inside a chapter.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF, A TALLY-BOARD, A KNIFE AND A BARROW.** Was asked, on the forty-second day of the Bare Month, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, what the column is for, and answered, once, in seven words, in his own words. **The answer is on a page in Chapter 0357 and in no file in this repository, and no file any phase produces may print it.** He has not been asked anything since and has not wanted to be. On the seventy-eighth day of that month a wall told a room that nobody has to be told what a form is for, and he refused it out loud in about four seconds in six words, and nobody improved on the six words and nobody thanked him. On the seventy-ninth day he said out loud on a wharf that the price of a name at the top of a page is his and not the woman whose name it is and not the woman who wrote it. **He has a second figure in his head that is on no page and he announced it twice and printed it nowhere, and no chapter puts a column against either figure.** He is right about most of what he says and nobody in this city has ever asked him which of the two numbers he believes.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER, HER SECOND SLATE.** Asked the question above, and afterwards said out loud in front of about nine people what she was going to write and what she was not, and the not was the whole of it, **and the first edition of that line reproduced ten consecutive words of her speech from the page verbatim, which has been paraphrased here instead: it is not the held sentence, it is the other speech in the same room on the same day, and a state file that copies a mouth is doing the thing this volume is about.** Entered one line in her register on the eightieth day of that month, a Thursday, with the day 391 on it — the seventy-sixth, a Sunday — and nothing under the day, and the third column left empty on purpose, and the second column empty because nothing has come back. **She has never put a name under that day and nobody is going to.** The two women of about twenty-nine are never in one room in any of the fifty chapters and nothing in this volume settles whether they are one woman and nothing may.

**THE OTHER WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE, TAMSIN QUILL, A FOLDED SHEET OF ROAD WITH ABOUT NINE PLACES ON IT, FOLDED THE SAME WAY EVERY TIME.** Took it out of her apron on the eighty-fourth day of that month on the wharf boards, opened it, folded it the same four ways and put it back, and gave it to nobody. The fourth crease has gone soft. **Her sheet and the sheet thirty years old in a tin are never in one room.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WITH A BARROW OF BOARDS — HE IS A DIFFERENT MAN AND A CHAPTER MAY NOT MERGE THEM.** He came up a lane and took nine boards off a wall on day 347 and left about four inches of dry stone at the foot of it. **He is not the wharf man, nothing on a page has ever put them in one room, and a descriptor is not a decision.** `outline/volume-08.md` §20.9 is that decision and the close did not reopen it.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE, A BOARD, NINETEEN RATES IN FOUR MARKETS.** **He is right, in Chapter 0393, in about four seconds, in one turn of speech, about what a name at the top of a page does, and nobody told him he was wrong and nobody told him he was right and the page went anyway.** He took a name off his own board in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and has never said so to anybody, and saying so out loud in a room in daylight is what refusing the wall's rule cost him in Chapter 0340. **His counter is on a page at `chapter-0390.md:35` and no chapter of Volume 08 prints a room count for him.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL, A PIECE OF CHALK HE DOES NOT USE, THE NINTH LINE.** The word went on the register-keeper's second slate in chalk on day 310 and came off it in chalk with a cloth on day 342, thirty-two days later, and the chalk in his own inside breast pocket has not been taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day 298. **The ninth line did not move, was not dated, was not read aloud, was not taken down, was not thanked for and was not resolved, and he asked in advance not to be thanked and is not thanked.** He is on no roster in this city at his own request. **He is the one figure in this volume — *about two hundred rooms* — that is on a page in his mouth at `chapter-0374.md:33`, in a speech paragraph that carries no descriptor in it, and it is routed to a review and is not available to anybody new.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT, A FOLDING KNIFE ON HIS KNEE.** Said a figure out loud in a room in Chapter 0367 and again in Chapter 0375, and the nine that goes with it is the same man's in both chapters. **He is one of the five people the figure *about two hundred rooms* is on, and he is the one instance in this volume where the same man said the same figure in two chapters, and that is the closest thing in fifty days to a person being consistent.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING, A SATCHEL WITH A FLAP, AND HIS FIVE SHEETS IN THIS FLOOD OF WHICH HE CARRIED FOUR IN A COAT.** Wrote the notices and opened none of them and is not going to. **He is one of the five the figure *about two hundred rooms* is on, in narration, at `chapter-0369.md:13`.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN, A LENGTH OF NEW ROPE HE HAS NEVER USED.** Paid money for half a holding about forty years ago and cannot show anybody where it is, and is right about most of what he says. **He is standing in front of a fence he cannot move and has not tried to move. He is not asked where the fence is in any of the fifty chapters, is not told, and is not defeated. The new question about the fence went to a room and not to him. He does not leave, he is not converted, and he is not happy.** He was in ten rooms in about a fortnight and was asked one question in none of them, and that figure is on a page at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:7` and not in a plan. **REPAIRED BY THE VOLUME 09 REVIEW REPAIR PASS, AND THE REPAIR IS NAMED IN PLACE BECAUSE A SILENT CORRECTION IS THE THIRD VERSION OF THE SAME FAULT: this line said *eleven* rooms, and the eleven is the man of about thirty-nine's, on a page at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35`, and his ten is at `chapter-0388.md:33`. The two men are two men and this file now carries each man's own count and neither man's other one. Volume 07's own close gives this man nine rooms in about a fortnight for Volume 07, at `state/volume-07-close.md:73`.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, A HANDCART, A HAZEL MALLET WHOSE HEAD IS BOUND IN THE CORD, AND A COIL OF ROPE.** Put the fence up, nine posts on day 348, four on day 350, the last three on day 353, and nobody sent him. **He said out loud in a lane that he has begun to want to know what his own work is for and that he is not going to find out, and he is not going to be asked.** He and the man of about fifty-seven were in one room for about four hours on the eighty-first day of that month and neither of them was asked the question the room was given.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE, A BARROW AND A HURDLE OF HAZEL RODS, AND A BAR HE REPLACED AT A GATE.** Has walked the four-hundred-mile road four times in nine years and had nobody on it until three days before day 400. **Came up the shut road on the eighty-fifth day of that month, put his barrow down on the low side of the fence, saw three people on the road below him, did not go after them, did not ask, and is not going to. He is the last image and he is not going to be asked about it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-FIVE, WHO HAS NINE WORDS OF HIS OWN IN THE INSIDE OF HIS COAT.** Has not said them in three rooms and has a page of his own that nobody in this city can answer for him about.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-ONE, A PEN WITH THE NIB GROUND NARROW.** Was asked once, in a room, on the sixtieth day of that month, whether it will go, and that is the only question anybody in this flood has asked her about herself, and nobody is going to ask her a second one. **She is not asked what she is for, she has stopped waiting to be told, and no chapter of Volume 08 made her start again.** She is two days down a road on the seventy-eighth day of that month and is on no page for it.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE, A TIN.** Carried the tin a mile and it was empty, three times in this volume. **Asked the last question this series has asked, on the thirty-first day of that month, a Thursday, and got nothing back, and asked the new one on the eighty-first day of that month, a Friday, fifty days later, in the same room, and got nothing back.** The two questions rhyme on purpose and neither is answered and the rhyme is not improved on. **There is no form in four hundred miles for being the person who asks.** The condition she attached to the sheet in the tin on the fifth day of that month is hers and nobody four hundred miles can take a condition off a person.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, A DOOR SHE IS NOT ASKED TO OPEN, A HOUSEHOLD OF ABOUT NINE.** Said no on a step before anybody was asked, on the forty-first day of that month, standing. The fourth place on the return went out empty and went out correct. **She is not the woman of Chapter 0266 and the finding that a household's days are not a rota belongs to the one who said it first and stays hers, and her days were not asked for a second time in any of the fifty chapters.** She is not the woman of about fifty-two who digs; he is eleven miles the other way and was not asked anything about the mark in a field in any of them.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS, A ROD HE DOES NOT CARRY.** Not in any chapter of Volume 08 and not asked anything. **The mark was not measured, dug, counted or probed in any of the fifty chapters and it has no word on any page in the world.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY AT A SHUT GATE, A BRASS KEY ON A TARRED CORD.** Wrote a date on a post in chalk and did not rub anything out, and said the date out loud to the post and not to a person. **Never once asked anything by anybody.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN AND THE STORE'S BOARD.** Washes it every morning and has decided not to wash the wall above it. **The board has stood unwritten since day 225, its figures are those of day 224, and it is the only instrument this book has said has not lied to anybody.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FORTY-EIGHT IN A FOURTH HOUSE AT NINE ELMS, THE GIRL OF SEVENTEEN, THE KEEPER OF THE REDROOT GATE, THE REGENT, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN IN `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0293.md`, THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-TWO ON THE BANK.** All live, all untouched by Volume 08, all at `state/volume-07-close.md` §2 and §4 and `outline/volume-08.md` §16 at their length. **The man of about thirty-seven is a real person on a page and is not the board-trader, and a chapter may not give him a trade, a board, a market or a refusal.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE, BY PATH: `workspace/volume-09/outline/PROMPT.md`, and it is a VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE and not a batch and not a close. It writes no chapter. It decides the cast, and no name and no place may arrive by narration, and the instrument is a document that arrives and is read out in a room in daylight with a date on the door, four days' notice, nothing at the top of it, to a place and not to a house.**
