## VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE — the cast as it stands for days 401 to 450, and this block wins over everything above it

**A Volume 09 outline phase has run and wrote no chapter. The plan is `outline/volume-09.md` and the decisions are in `outline/series.md`. Nothing in this file is a page: every person below is a proposal, and a proposal becomes a person when a chapter puts them in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, and says what they do. No name and no place arrives by narration in this book.**

**NO NAME AND NO PLACE ARRIVES BY NARRATION. IT IS CHECKED FOR A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR, BY A PERSON. AND THE INSTRUMENT FOR VOLUME 09 IS A DOCUMENT: a second sheet comes up the road on day 401 with a heading and four places under it — a place, a line, a day, and a person — and is read out on day 405 by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, who says out loud what it is for before anybody asks. And there is no eighth notice in this flood and this volume sends none: the page is `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0389.md:13`, and every room in the volume is a room about nine people were already in.**

**THE ONE NEW PERSON THIS VOLUME ADDS, and it is one and not two, because a volume whose instrument is a sheet read out to the nine who were already in the room does not need a room full of strangers.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FOUR. A length of blue thread tied round her wrist, which is how she has always known a length by, and which is on no page before Chapter 0420.** Measured at **three occurrences in one file across all four hundred chapter files, at zero in Volume 07's fifty files and at zero in Volume 08's fifty files**, and taken from a published set of forty-seven descriptors that stand at zero in both of the last two volumes, because the twelve the close published as a pool all already have people on a page. **She is put on a page by being asked, in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, where her line is, on day 420, and that is the whole of the instrument and the whole of her first appearance. She answers correctly. Her line is one of the four. One of the four comes back moved and hers is the one that has moved, and nobody is ever going to ask her about it, because a chapter that asks her is a chapter that resolves it. She is in no chapter of Volume 09 after the one in which she is asked, and her descriptor may not be given to anybody else.**

**THE RETURNING CAST, with the second handle beside the age and the trade and never instead of it, and the same handle in every chapter the person is in after the one that invents it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN, A LENGTH OF NEW ROPE HE HAS NEVER USED.** Paid money for half a holding about forty years ago and cannot show anybody where it is, and is right about most of what he says, and is standing in front of a fence he cannot move and has not tried. **NOT ASKED, NOT TOLD, NOT DEFEATED, NOT LEAVING, NOT HAPPY.** On day 432 he reads one of the four lines on the sheet out loud because it is in front of him and he is reading a page, and he does not say it is his, and nobody asks him and nobody in the room may suggest it. **The rope is not used in this volume and no chapter has him cut it or take it up.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE WHO FARMS THE HALF OF THE HOLDING, A SPADE.** He is on the other side of the fence from the man of about fifty-seven, the two have never agreed where the line is, neither is asked, and **no chapter of the volume puts a line between them on a page or says whose line it is.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR WHO DIGS SOMEBODY ELSE'S DITCH NINE HUNDRED PACES OFF AND IS NOT PAID, A SPADE HE IS NOT PAID FOR.** On a page in one file of Volume 07. In this volume for four lines, and is not asked anything.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, A HAZEL MALLET WITH ITS HEAD BOUND IN CORD AND A COIL OF ROPE.** He put the fence up, he was not sent, and he is not going to be asked why. **The coil in his cart is a different rope from the man of about fifty-seven's and has been used and is not new, and no card may join the two.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE WHO WALKS THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD, A HURDLE HE MADE AND A BAR HE REPLACED.** He has walked it four times in nine years, he had nobody on it until day 397, and on day 410 he puts his barrow down on the low side of the fence and looks down the road at three people going away from him and does not go after them and does not ask and is not going to.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL, A PIECE OF CHALK HE DOES NOT USE, IN AN INSIDE BREAST POCKET, NOT TAKEN OUT SINCE DAY 298.** He wrote the ninth line nine miles up the road, last, in a harder piece of chalk, on purpose, and has refused five times to say whether it works and has asked in advance not to be thanked and is not thanked. **On day 428 he is asked what a line is, by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, and what he says is the name of this volume, in four to eight words, on that page and in no file. On day 438, ten days later, in the same room, he refuses the volume's one rule in about four seconds, and what it costs him is his own ninth line, because the rule tells that room that his line is not a claim either and that the reason nothing has moved it is a road with nobody on it. Nobody improves on what he says and nobody thanks him. A chapter may not have him say he knows what he said and may not make the ninth line and the four lines on the sheet the same line.** The full form of the day the chalk went into his pocket is printed at full length in the chapters that carry it and is never shortened to a relative phrase.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF, A TALLY-BOARD, A KNIFE AND A BARROW.** He was asked on day 357 what the column is for, for the first time in nine years, and answered in his own words, and what he said is the name of the last volume and is on a page in Chapter 0357 and in no file; and he refused that volume's one rule on day 393 in six words, and nobody improved on the six words and nobody thanked him. **Nothing in Volume 09 repeats, restates, improves on or puts those two acts in one room on one day, and the six words and the seven are not in any file this phase wrote.** In this volume he is right about a sheet with a person in it in about four seconds and is thanked by nobody. **He is not the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347 and the two are two people and §16.13 of the plan is the decision.** The figure he sends is not the figure he has and the figure he has is on no page and is printed nowhere.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD, A BOARD.** He took a name off that board in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and has never said so to anybody, and he is right about a column in about four seconds and is thanked by nobody and argued with by nobody. **He is thirty-nine and not thirty-seven. In this volume he is right about the sheet in about four seconds and is thanked by nobody and is not defeated and is not going to be.** His right hand lies flat over the face of his own slate in any room he is in and nobody is shown what is on the face of it.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT, A DOCUMENT INSIDE THE COAT AND NO LOCAL LANGUAGE.** He is the office's man and he is why that office is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated. He said out loud in Volume 08 that he does not have the local language and is not going to ask anybody to slow down. **He carries the second sheet up the road and he is in this volume's rooms and he reads his own document and does not describe the room where anything got decided, and the nine sentences about a return that are his are not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the fifty.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER, HER SECOND SLATE.** She reads the sheet out on day 405 and says out loud what it is for before anybody asks. **On day 425, a Saturday, she decides out loud, in that room, naming the cost first, that the third place is left empty**, and the reason is not that it is the smaller of the two. On day 409 she tells about nine people that she is not going to be the person who decides this and that somebody is going to have to. **The register's third column has been empty on purpose for fifty days of Volume 08 and is empty on purpose for fifty days of this one, and the second slate on the shelf behind it has a date on it and nothing under it and is never picked up, never turned over and never written on. She is not Tamsin Quill and the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHOSE HOUSEHOLD'S DAYS WERE ASKED, A DOOR SHE IS NOT ASKED TO OPEN.** She refused the fourth place of a return in a kitchen in Volume 08 and is on a page and returns. **One of the four lines on the sheet is hers and she knows it before anybody has said so, and no chapter of this volume asks her for her days again and no chapter puts the sentence about a household's days into her mouth or improves on it. She is not the woman of Chapter 0266 and that finding belongs to the one who said it first.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE, A TIN.** She holds a folded sheet thirty years old in a tin with a condition attached to it that is hers, and the tin is not in this volume and is not opened and nobody may un-condition it. **She asks this series' question for the third time on day 445, a Friday, in the room over a market, in daylight, with a date on the door, to the room and not to anybody: *what is a line for, on a page, when the only thing anybody will use it for is to move it.*** Nobody answers it. About four of the nine had an answer and about four said nothing. **She asked the first on day 346 and the second on day 396 and got nothing both times, and the three rhyme on purpose and a chapter may not say that they rhyme and may not improve the rhyme and may not say that the rhyme is the point.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-ONE WITH THE PEN WITH THE NIB GROUND NARROW.** One of the four. She went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391 and at day 401 she is ten days out and at day 450 fifty-nine, both derived from day 391. **She is in no chapter of Volume 09, nobody in this volume is going after her, she is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and a party of four is four. She has already stopped waiting to be told what she is for and may not be made to start again.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS, A ROD HE DOES NOT CARRY.** He looked along a mark in a field from the end of it on day 344, did not touch it, and said one sentence that is not an answer, and the sentence is on no page and is not going to be. **He is not asked anything in Volume 09 and is not in it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING, A SATCHEL WITH A FLAP.** He carried five notices in a coat in Volume 07 and opened none of them, and a return in a satchel in Volume 08 and did not open it, and this sheet on day 401 and does not open that one either. **That is three things in two volumes and the third is the last of them in this flood, because there is not going to be an eighth sheet that goes past a man. A card may not turn him into the man who reads what he carries, because a man who carries things and reads them is a different man and is not available to anybody.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-THREE WHO KEEPS A ROOM OVER A MARKET.** On a page at `chapters/volume-07/chapter-0348.md`, at two occurrences in one file across four hundred chapter files. **HE IS NOT A NEW MAN AND HE IS NOT IN THIS VOLUME. What this volume uses is the room, and the room is a room and not a man, and the man who keeps it stays off the page. A card that treats him as a new person in a volume that is set in the room he keeps has merged a man with a building, and this paragraph is the decision.**

**TAMSIN QUILL — her second handle is a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time, the fourth crease gone soft.** She is not the register-keeper and the folded sheet is not the sheet thirty years old in a tin and the two sheets are never in one room and never joined. **She says once in her own mouth, in about nine words, that a sheet that says where a line runs is the plainest instrument of a claim this flood has produced, and it is not improved on and not said twice. The consent fracture is not mended and nobody may mend it, apologise for it, refuse an apology for it, forgive it or ask for it to be forgiven, and the fifth condition is not given.**

**ADRIAN VALE, STAGE 2, A WITNESS, IN THE DOING, ALL FIFTY DAYS.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once across the volume, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the fifty files, and he is aged nowhere in it. **He is named in nine of the fifty chapters — 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447 — and in no chapter in any form in the other forty-one, and in eight of the nine he is in a room reading a page. Chapter 0436 is the ninth and it is the chapter in which he reads nothing: a Wednesday morning on the boards of a salt wharf with a stack higher than a man, a strip of clear board about as long as a cart, a tally-board and a knife, and nothing on any of them that anybody has to fill in. Nobody asks him anything, nobody declines to ask him anything, and he works something out and the chapter does not say he was right. No chapter may be opened or moved to make room for it and a batch may not add a tenth.**

**THE CROWDED FOUR, WHICH MAY NOT BE GIVEN TO A NEW PERSON BY ANYBODY, ALL FOUR REPRODUCING ON 400 FILES: a woman of about thirty-four at 362 in 147, a man of about thirty-four at 341 in 139, a man of about forty-four at 162 in 85, a man of about thirty-nine at 177 in 93.** The man of about thirty-four and the man of about thirty-nine are returning people in this volume and their ages are settled and they may not be given a second descriptor. **A man of about forty-four is not used for anybody in this volume at all**, at zero in both of the last two volumes and 162 in 85 across the four hundred, and a descriptor that is at zero in one volume and enormous in the seven before it is a descriptor with a person attached to it that no page has ever named.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN IS NOT AVAILABLE TO ANYBODY.** At zero in Volume 07's fifty and Volume 08's fifty, at six occurrences in one file across the four hundred, and that one file is `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0293.md`, and he is a real person on a page and is a question-carrier and is not the board-trader. **No chapter of this volume may give him a trade, a board, a market, a line, a sheet or a refusal. The man who trades on the Open Hand board is thirty-nine.**

**AND A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY IS NOT AVAILABLE TO ANYBODY EITHER, AND THE REASON IS A READER AND NOT AN INSTRUMENT.** He stands at one occurrence in one file across the four hundred, and the published descriptor definition exists to stop *man of about thirty* being returned inside *man of about thirty-nine*, and **a figure a person cannot see on a page quickly is a figure a person will see wrong on a page quickly.** No new person in this volume and no chapter of it may print the bare tens word without a units word.

**THE FIGURE *ABOUT TWO HUNDRED ROOMS* IS NOT AVAILABLE TO A NEW PERSON AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME MAY GIVE IT TO ANYBODY.** It is on the pages of Volume 08 and belongs, on those pages as they stand, to Adrian Vale in narration, to a woman who keeps a sheet in an apron in a mouth, to the man of about forty-eight twice in a mouth, to the man of about thirty-one in narration, and to one speech paragraph in Chapter 0374 that carries no descriptor at all. **Five people and a woman already hold it, the state layer once said it was at zero in all fifty chapters, and that was a false zero; this run measures ten occurrences in six files against the close's ten in nine chapters, and the attribution is carried unpaid for a review repair pass and is not this phase's.**

**THE ONES WHO ARE NOT IN THIS VOLUME AND WHO ARE NOT ASKED ABOUT ANYTHING IN IT: the woman of about fifty at a shut gate, a brass key on a tarred cord, who has never once been asked anything by anybody and whom no chapter of this volume sends anybody to; the girl of seventeen, who is on no roll and is in no chapter of Volumes 06, 07 or 08 and whom this volume does not fetch; the keeper of the Redroot gate, whose year is not named, whose days are not counted and who is not used; the woman of about forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms; the Regent, nine hundred miles away and not defeated; the man of about thirty-two on the bank; the man of about thirty-one on the bank.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE, BY PATH: `workspace/volume-09/batch-0001/PROMPT.md`, and it is a BATCH and not an outline. It writes Chapters 0401 to 0410, days 401 to 410, ten chapters of finished prose, and it decides nothing and invents nobody.**

**AND THE CO-PRESENCE FIGURE, CORRECTED IN PLACE IN THE BLOCK ABOVE'S OWN TERMS, because a state file that publishes a false zero about a merge is the most expensive kind of false zero in this repository. THE STANDING PROHIBITION IS: no paragraph may carry the man of about thirty-four and a woman of about thirty-four together. `state/volume-08-close.md` §7.7 publishes it as at zero word-bounded and twenty unbounded. ON THE VOLUME 09 OUTLINE PHASE'S RUN NEITHER REPRODUCES: ONE word-bounded in Volume 08's fifty files, at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0354.md:7`, and THIRTEEN across the four hundred, and ELEVEN and THREE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-SIX on the unbounded reading. The thirteen were read and are NOT thirteen merges, and the two people named in the one in Volume 08 are the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall and the woman of about thirty-four who keeps the stall nearest the water end, and they are two people and neither is the other, and a paragraph naming both in separate sentences while both are in the room is what a room looks like in this book. `chapters/volume-04/chapter-0157.md:59` IS a speech paragraph carrying both descriptors and is the shape the prohibition is about. THE RESTATEMENT, at `outline/volume-09.md` §20.9: no SPEECH paragraph of Volume 09 may carry both descriptors, a narration line may name both where both are in the room and only where they stand in separate sentences and the line cannot be read as one person, and a card may not put the two in one exchange at all.**

# VOLUME 09 OUTLINE — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the authoritative block, and it wins over every block above it

**ONE CAST FIGURE IN THIS FILE WAS WRONG AND IS REPAIRED IN PLACE AT LINE 913, NAMED IN PLACE, AND THE REST OF THIS PASS IS ABOUT FIGURES RATHER THAN PEOPLE. The full record is at the foot of `state/current.md`.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE ARE TWO MEN AND THEIR ROOM COUNTS ARE THEIRS SEPARATELY, and this file carried the wrong one for four volumes' worth of readers.** **The fifty-seven: ten rooms in about a fortnight, asked one question in none of them, on a page at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0364.md:7`; and nine rooms in about a fortnight in Volume 07, at `state/volume-07-close.md:73`. The thirty-nine: eleven at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0390.md:35` and ten at `chapter-0388.md:33`, and the nine, ten and eleven sequence for him is on this file already at line 791 and is right. Neither man holds the other's count and no chapter of Volume 09 prints a room count at all.**

**THE FOUR SECONDS, AND WHICH MAN, AND IT IS NOT THE SAME MAN IN TWO CHAPTERS.** **Chapter 0406 is the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board: he is the point of view, he is right about the third place in about four seconds, nobody tells him he is right, nobody tells him he is wrong, and the chapter does not say he was right. Chapter 0408 is the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf: he is the point of view, the four seconds are his, the man of about thirty-nine is on those boards for about an hour as the marked voice and is not the man who is right that morning, and nobody thanks either of them.** A card may not merge those two men and may not spend the same four seconds twice, and the beat may not stand twice in one chapter.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — the two figures a card got wrong, both now measured and both with the reading published.** He is in Chapters 0407 and 0409 of Volume 09's first batch and is asked nothing in either. **Word-bounded and case-sensitive on `man of about thirty-four`, he stands at 17 occurrences in 16 paragraphs across 10 files in Volume 07's fifty, at 58 in 58 across 31 files in Volume 08's fifty, and at 341 in 334 across 139 files across the 400; the longer descriptor carrying his trade stands at 7 in 6 in Volume 07 and 256 in 118 across the 400; an unbounded search returns 24 occurrences in 23 paragraphs across 11 files for Volume 07 and 69 in 68 across 32 for Volume 08.** The *twenty-seven paragraphs across eight chapters* that a card published is Volume 06's figure, was already repaired once at `state/current.md` item 7, and is not re-imported. **He has been on a page in every volume of this flood since Volume 03 and any line saying otherwise is struck.**

**AND THE MEN THIS PASS DID NOT TOUCH, so that a reader knows the boundary of it: the man of about thirty-seven is still not available to anybody; the man of about thirty-three who keeps a room is still a man and not a room; the two men of about thirty-eight are still two men; *woman of about fifty-four* is still this volume's one new person and is still put on a page on day 420 by being asked; the man of about sixty-three and the man of about sixty-four are still on the two sides of a fence nobody is asked about; and the woman of about sixty-nine, the woman of about twenty-one, the fourth of the four, the woman of about fifty at the shut gate and the girl of seventeen are all still in no chapter of this volume and are not going to be asked about anything by anybody.**

**AND THE TWO FAULTS ROUTED INTO THIS FILE, BOTH STILL UNPAID AND NEITHER TOUCHED BY THIS PASS: the unpaired straight quotation mark on line 223, which is still line 223; and the six-word refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, which is on a page once and is reproduced verbatim inside bold marks at `bible/power-system.md:1686`, so that two records saying it is in no other file are false. Neither is repaired here and neither is rerouted.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0001 — the cast as it stands after days 401 to 410, and this block wins over every block above it

**A batch has run and wrote ten chapters. Everything below is on a page in `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0401.md` through `chapter-0410.md` and in no other chapter of this batch, and no descriptor below was changed, given a second figure, or taken away, and no new person was put on a page.** Volume 09's one new person is still a woman of about fifty-four and she still arrives on day 420 by being asked where her line is, and she is in none of these ten.

**AND THE THING THIS BATCH HAD TO PUBLISH ABOUT A DESCRIPTOR, because a descriptor is not a decision and a decision about one is not a batch's:** the plan enumerates the four places under the sheet's heading as *a place, a line, a day, and a person* and states in nine places that the third of the four is a person. **The printed order on the page is A PLACE, A LINE, A PERSON, AND A DAY, and the third is a person, and that is what Chapters 0402 and 0405 put on the page.** Full statement and the reason is in `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §3.11.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING. A satchel with a flap.** He put a sheet on a table in a room over a market on day 401 and did not open it, and he said one true thing out loud about why, and the reason is his and it is short and it was not improved on. **He has carried seven things with somebody else's writing on them in this flood — five in a coat, a return in a satchel on the three hundred and fifty-first day of this flood, and this sheet — and has opened none of the seven, and he said the figure out loud on a page in Chapter 0401, and the last of them is the last sheet of paper in this flood that has gone past him.** He did not tell anybody what was on it. He is in Chapter 0401 and nowhere else in this batch.

**ADRIAN VALE, STAGE 2, A WITNESS, IN THE DOING, ALL TEN DAYS.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once across the ten files, nobody offers him a workway, the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten, and he is aged nowhere in them. **He is named in Chapters 0402 and 0405, in nine places, and in no chapter in any form in the other eight, and in both he is in a room reading a page.** In Chapter 0402 he read a sheet with a heading and four places ruled under it, and the four of them are a place, a line, a person and a day in the order they are printed, and he set the first finger of his left hand on the paper a finger's width back from the edge of the third column and did not touch it, and then he said one true thing about the sheet out loud, which was a fact about the paper and not an opinion about the four places, and he stopped one step short of the rest of it and nobody in that room asked him for the rest. In Chapter 0405 he stood at the shelf behind the table and read the second slate where it lay, without lifting it and without turning it over, and what was on it was a date and nothing under the date. **He is not in Chapter 0436, which is a later chapter's, and nothing in these ten warms it up, and the volume's list of nine is 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447 and this batch added nothing to it and took nothing off it.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER. Her second slate.** She read the sheet out on day 405, in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, to about nine people who had all been up those nine steps before, in the order the four places are printed on, stopping at each one long enough for the people at the back to hear it, and she said out loud what it was for before anybody asked. **She is thanked by nobody in either chapter she speaks in and neither chapter says she was right.** On day 409 she said out loud in front of about nine people that she is not going to be the person who decides what the third place is and that somebody is going to have to, and she said the cost first, and she did not name the day. **The second slate is on its shelf in five of the ten chapters and is read in two and is never picked up, never turned over and never written on, and it carries a date and nothing under the date.** Her book's third column has been empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days, and she is the one who has kept it empty and has never been asked about it. **She is not Tamsin Quill, the two of them are never in one room in these ten chapters, and nothing here settles whether they are one woman.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL. A piece of chalk he does not use, in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day two hundred and ninety-eight.** He is in Chapters 0407 and 0409 and is asked nothing in either and is thanked in neither, and he has asked in advance, in a lane, not to be thanked. **The full form of the day the chalk went into that pocket is printed at full length in Chapter 0407, in the chapter lead and again in his own mouth, and Chapter 0409 names the chalk with no date at all rather than shortening the date to a relative phrase.** He said three things across the batch — to a man washing a board at the end of a market, to the room over a market, and once from the back of that room — and in the last of them he said that whoever it is can have the chalk off him. **The ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not taken down in any of the ten, and whether it works stays unanswered, and the two acts this volume gives him are on day 428 and day 438 and neither of them is this batch's.** He is thirty-four and not given a second figure.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD. A board, and a slate of his own with his right hand over the face of it.** He is in Chapters 0406 and 0408. **In Chapter 0406 he is the point of view, he is right about the third place in four words, and nobody in that room told him he was right and nobody told him he was wrong, and the chapter does not say he was right.** In Chapter 0408 he is on the boards of a salt wharf for about an hour as the marked voice, and he is not the man who is right that morning, and nobody thanked him there either. **His right hand lay over the face of his own slate the whole time he was in that room and did not move off it once, and nobody was shown what is on the face of it, and he has not shown anybody and was not going to.** He is thirty-nine and not thirty-seven. He is not thanked in either chapter and he is not defeated and is not going to be.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF. A tally-board, a knife, a barrow, and a strip of clear board about as long as a cart at the bottom of a stack higher than a man.** He is in Chapters 0403 and 0408 and is the point of view in Chapter 0408. **In Chapter 0403 he worked the boards for about two hours with the man who brings the round standing at the bottom of them and not one word of it was aimed at the other one, and in Chapter 0408 he worked them for about as long as it takes to fill a pail and then stopped, and he did not go up the nine steps, and what he said was four seconds long and it was true, and nobody on those boards told him he was right and nobody told him he was wrong.** **The figure he sends is not the figure he has, and NEITHER IS PRINTED IN ANY OF THE TEN: Chapter 0408 states the difference in words and prints no number at all, and the figure he sends is therefore absent from this batch as well as the figure he has.** **He is not the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, and that man is nowhere in this batch, and the two were not merged.** Neither of his two acts in the last volume is repeated, restated, improved on or put in one room on one day in either of his two chapters.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT. A document inside the coat, and no local language.** He came up nine steps on day 403 and said once in that room that he does not have the local language and did not say it twice and was not arranged for afterwards. He took a document out of the coat and did not open it for about a quarter of an hour and then opened it and read it and shut it and told nobody what was in it, and nobody asked him. **He said one true thing about the round and not about the sheet, and the true thing was how long the round takes, and it is a count of days.** **He did not describe the room where anything got decided, because he has not been in it, and the nine sentences that are his are not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in any of the ten.** He is not defeated and he is not leaving.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN. A length of new rope he has never used, over the back of his chair.** He is in Chapters 0402 and 0410 and is asked nothing in either, and he is not told anything and he does not leave and he is not happy. **In Chapter 0402 he stood at the end of the bench of a cold room for about four hours with the rope over the back of his chair and said one true thing out loud, and nobody in that room told him he was right and nobody told him he was wrong, and the chapter does not say he was right either. In Chapter 0410 he was at a fence along the top of a shut road out of this city and said four things, all four of them to a barrow and none of them to the man of about fifty-nine, and not one of them was about where the fence is.** **Nobody asks him where the fence is, nobody tells him, he does not move the fence, and he does not touch the rope. The rope is over the back of a chair in a room in this city in three of the four chapters that name it and is nowhere near that road in the fifth, and it has still never been used.** He put his hand flat on the top of the sixteenth post on his way past and did not stop. **The count of sixteen is on a page in Chapter 0410 and was printed once in the batch, and no chapter of the ten prints a room count for him or for anybody.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE WHO WALKS THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD. A hurdle he made, a bar he replaced, and a barrow that is empty going up and empty coming down.** He came up the shut road on day 410 for the fifth time in nine years, put his barrow down on the low side of the fence, and looked down the road at three people going away from him, and did not go after them and did not ask. **He said out loud one true thing about the three on that road and it is the same arrangement he has said before on that road, and the chapter does not repeat it and does not improve on it, and the repetition is declared as a motif and not as a fault.** The figure for the three is ten days and it is printed once in the batch with day 391 named in the same sentence, and it does not go into a mouth.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, A HAZEL MALLET WITH ITS HEAD BOUND IN CORD AND A COIL OF ROPE.** His handcart came up the same road behind the man of about fifty-nine in Chapter 0410 and the two of them had not asked each other anything for about a fortnight and neither of them was going to start. **The coil in that cart is a different rope from the man of about fifty-seven's and has been used and is not new, and no chapter of the ten joins the two.** He is named in Chapter 0410 and in no other of the ten and he was not sent for and was not asked.

**TAMSIN QUILL. A folded sheet of road with about nine places on it, folded the same way every time, the fourth crease gone soft.** She is in Chapter 0404 and in no other of the ten. **She said once, in her own mouth, that a page that says where a line runs is the plainest claim in this flood, and it is not improved on and it is not said twice in this volume.** She did not take the sheet out of her apron, and it did not leave the front of it, and it is not the sheet thirty years old in a tin and the two were never in one room. **She said out loud in a kitchen with nobody in it that a thing you know and do not say is a thing you have chosen, and she chose it out loud in a room with a date on the door, and she is not going to un-choose it in a kitchen.** The consent fracture is not mended: nobody apologised in that chapter, nobody forgave anything in it, nothing was mended in it, she is not going to go and find him, and the fifth condition was not given and was not hinted at. She said four sentences to the man of about thirty-eight on the wharf in an hour and none of the four was about the sheet that was read out the next morning.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN, A BUCKET AND A RAG.** He washes the store's board every morning and has washed it every morning since the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood, which is the last day anything was written on it, and **he decided about a month ago not to wash the wall above it, and he said so once, in a market, to a man with a stall board, and the man asked him what he decided that on and he said he decided it on his own.** He is in Chapter 0407 and in no other of the ten. **A board nobody has written on is the only instrument anybody in this city has said out loud has not lied to anybody, and that sentence is the man of about thirty-four's, it is on a page in Chapter 0407, and he was thanked by nobody for saying it.**

**AND THE ONES WHO ARE NOT IN THIS BATCH AND WHO WERE NOT ASKED ABOUT ANYTHING IN IT.** The woman of about fifty-two, whose household's days were asked once and refused the fourth place in its own kitchen: **this batch deliberately kept her out of its chapters so that the day she is in a room, which is day 411, is not spent early, and a household appears in Chapter 0406 unnamed and is asked nothing.** The man of about thirty-eight's other — the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347 — is in no chapter of this batch. The woman of about twenty-one with the pen with the nib ground narrow is ten days down a road and is not asked about anything. The fourth of the four is not asked about anything. The woman of about fifty at a shut gate, the girl of seventeen, the keeper of the Redroot gate, the woman of about forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms, the Regent, the man of about thirty-two on the bank and the man of about thirty-one on the bank are all in no chapter of this batch and were not sent for.

**AND THE DESCRIPTORS THIS BATCH DID NOT USE, published because a batch that keeps a list of what it avoided is doing the thing a record is for.** *Man of about thirty-seven* is at zero across the ten. **No woman of about thirty-four is in any of the ten chapters, so the co-presence prohibition is held at zero on the word-bounded reading and at zero on the unbounded reading and the narration-line relaxation at `outline/volume-09.md` §20.9 was not needed.** *Man of about forty-four* is at zero. ***About two hundred rooms* is at zero.** *Man of about thirty-one on the bank* and *man of about thirty-two on the bank* are at zero. **The man of about thirty-three who keeps a room over a market is not in this batch and is not a new man, and what this batch uses is the room, and the room is a room and not a man, and the man who keeps it stays off the page.**

**AND THE NEXT PHASE, BY PATH: `workspace/volume-09/batch-0002/PROMPT.md`, a batch and not an outline, writing Chapters 0411 to 0420, days 411 to 420, from a Saturday to a Monday, and deciding nothing.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0001 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the cast as it stands, and this block wins over every block above it

**A review of Batch 0001 found eleven faults, nine in its record and two on its pages, and the full list with the old figure beside every new one is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0001.md` §11. Exactly one of the eleven changes anything a later batch inherits about a person, and three sentences of Chapter 0410 changed their wording. No descriptor was changed, given a second figure or taken away, no new person was put on a page, and the volume's list of nine chapters Adrian Vale is in is untouched at 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447.**

**1. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING, and the one date about him that was wrong.** The return came up the road on day 351 and Chapter 0401 printed *the fifty-first day of this flood* in the two places that name it, so the page named day 51. **Both are now *the three hundred and fifty-first day of this flood*.** The seven things are unchanged: five in a coat, that return in a satchel, and the sheet, and he has opened none of the seven, and the last of them is the last sheet of paper in this flood that has gone past him. He is in Chapter 0401 and nowhere else in this batch and he is not in any of the next four.

**2. THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE AND THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, and the three sentences of Chapter 0410 that were lifted whole out of `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0400.md`.** The man of about fifty-nine's marked turn on the shut road, the sentence about the man of about fifty-seven walking that fence and getting sixteen both times, and the closing sentence in which the barrow comes up off the low side and goes back down the road — **all three are varied in place and every fact in them is kept: nine years on that road, three people on it, no question asked in any of the nine years, the figure said out loud to nobody, the hand flat on every post, sixteen both times, the man of about sixty who put the fence up and was not sent for, and the barrow that is empty going up and empty coming down.** The arrangement the man of about fifty-nine declares on that road is his card's declared motif and it is still the arrangement after the repair; **what is gone is that Chapter 0410 was carrying Chapter 0400's wording, and the run it made was the largest in the batch against the rest of the manuscript and this record's own figure for it was wrong by a factor of three.** The man of about sixty is still a bare tens word, still twice in Chapter 0410, and still published as a deviation at the batch record §3.10.

**3. THE FIGURES THAT WERE RE-MEASURED AND FOUND RIGHT, and a review that reports only what it corrected is half a record.** *Man of about thirty-seven* is at zero across the ten. No woman of about thirty-four is in any of the ten, on either reading. The full form of the day the chalk went into the inside breast pocket is printed at full length in Chapter 0407 in the lead and again in his mouth, and Chapter 0409 names the chalk with no date. Adrian Vale is named nine times across Chapters 0402 and 0405 and in no other chapter of the ten in any form, and the word *cooling* stands three times in the batch and all three are in Chapter 0401 on one line, two in the sentence that names the loss and one in the sentence that pays for it, **and a reader who runs a line count on that word will find one and conclude there is a fault, and there is not.** He has been thanked in none of the three chapters he speaks in, the ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not taken down, the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf is the same man in Chapters 0403 and 0408 and the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347 is in neither and is nowhere in this batch, and the woman of about fifty-four is still Volume 09's one new person and still arrives on day 420 and is still in none of these ten.

WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT TOUCH.** `state/character-state.md:223` is untouched and is still line 223, because every correction and every appended block in this file went in at the foot or as a same-line replacement below line 223. # VOLUME 09, BATCH 0002 — the cast as it stands after days 411 to 420, and this block wins over every block above it

**A batch has run and wrote ten chapters. Everything below is on a page in `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0411.md` through `chapter-0420.md` or is published in `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0002.md` with the instrument that produced it, and no descriptor below was changed, given a second figure, or taken away. Volume 09's one new person is on a page once, in Chapter 0420, and is not in any other of the ten, and no second person was added.**

**AND THE GAP IN THIS BATCH'S BRIEFING, because a cast record that hides where its cards were not is a cast record that is wrong about itself: the prompt told this batch to read its ten cards at `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0002.md` and that file does not exist on disk.** The cast below was reconstructed from `outline/volume-09.md` §7, §7.2, §7.3 and §20.6 and from the volume's own escalation sequence and from the ten chapters before it. The next batch is told to look for its own card file before it reads it and to say so in its record if it is not there.

**ADRIAN VALE, STAGE 2, A WITNESS, IN THE DOING, ALL TEN DAYS.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once across the ten files, nobody offers him a workway, the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten, and he is aged nowhere in them. **He is named in Chapter 0414 only, in three places, and in no chapter in any form in the other nine.** In Chapter 0414 he came up the nine steps at about the eighth hour, was not asked why he was in the room and was not asked for anything while he was in it, **read the register where it lay without lifting the book, without turning a page and without putting a hand on the frame of the shelf it lives on, and found the margin worn pale in a curve about the width of a thumb beside the third column and not beside the other two, and said one true thing about the paper and stopped one step short of it.** Nobody in that room asked him for the rest and nobody thanked him, **and the woman who keeps that book has never once asked him anything and is not going to start.** **He is not in Chapter 0436, which is not this batch's, and nothing in these ten warms it up, and the volume's list of nine is 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447 and this batch added nothing to it and took nothing off it.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER. Her second slate.** She is in six of the ten and speaks in four of them and is thanked by nobody in any of them and no chapter says she was right. **In Chapter 0416 she counted the four places under the sheet's heading out loud in front of about nine people — the first is a place and anybody can write it, the fourth is a day and anybody can write that too, the second is a line and a line is a thing a person can put down whether or not anybody is standing on either side of it — and said that it is a count and she has made it and that she is not saying what any of them is for, and she named no day.** **In Chapter 0419 she said, unprompted and to the table, that the room worked out about an hour ago that nobody in it has read the page the man in the very good coat brought them, and that none of them said anything for about forty minutes, and she is the first one in that room to admit the room was wrong and nobody thanked her for it.** **In Chapter 0420 she asked a woman of about fifty-four, standing up, in the local language, in daylight, with a date on the door, where her line is, and got an answer, and then said out loud in her own mouth that there is nowhere in four hundred miles to put an answer that somebody gave out loud in a room, and that it is a thing somebody in that room is going to have to think about, and that she is not going to complain about it in front of the woman she asked.** **Her second slate is not named in any of these ten chapters and was not picked up, not turned over and not written on, and her book's third column has been empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days.** She is not Tamsin Quill, the two of them are never in one room in these ten chapters, and nothing here settles whether they are one woman.

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO. A door she is not asked to open.** She is in Chapter 0411 and in no other of the ten. **She came up the nine steps, sat at the end of the bench furthest from the door, read the sheet from where she was sitting without being given it and without being asked to look at it, and said out loud, before anybody had said so, that there are going to be four lines on that page and one of the four is going to be hers, and that she has said it out loud first so that nobody in that room can stand there later on and say that she was asked.** Nobody asked her anything in that chapter or in any other of the ten, nobody told her she was right and nobody told her she was wrong, and she said out loud that nobody has ever asked her to open her own door and that she is not going to open it on a Saturday for a sheet of paper. **The finding that a household's days are not a rota is not in her mouth in any of these ten chapters and is not improved on and is nowhere in the batch.** She is not the woman of Chapter 0266 and nothing in these ten merges her with anybody.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL. A piece of chalk he does not use, in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day two hundred and ninety-eight.** He is present in seven of the ten and speaks in five, is asked nothing in any of them, and **is thanked in none of the five and argued with in none of the five.** **The full form of the day the chalk went into that pocket is printed at full length in Chapters 0413, 0416, 0417 and 0418 and no chapter of the ten shortens it to a relative phrase.** He says one true thing in Chapter 0413, that the page was folded twice and one of the two folds is the one down the third of the four places, and that he is not going to draw the second one and nobody in that room should let him; one in Chapter 0416, that he is not going to be the one that puts a mark on any of the four; one in Chapter 0418, addressed to a man at the other end of the same bench who has just said out loud that there is a first thing he is not going to say; one in Chapter 0419, that the man in the very good coat has been sitting ten feet from that page every morning all week and has not read one word of it, and that nobody in that room is going to arrange for the language and nobody is going to say it to him; and one flat interjection in Chapter 0420, that the second of the four places will take a line. **The ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud and not taken down in any of the ten, whether it works stays unanswered, nobody in any of the ten asks him what a line is, and nothing in these ten warms up the day he is asked it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN. A length of new rope he has never used, over the back of his chair.** He is in four of the ten — Chapters 0411, 0416, 0418 and 0420 — **is asked nothing in any of them, is not told anything in any of them, does not leave, and is not happy.** **Nobody asks him where the fence is, nobody tells him, he does not move the fence, and he does not touch the rope; the rope is over the back of his chair in three of the four and has still never been used.** **In Chapter 0418 he said, unasked, that he has not been asked one question in this flood and is not going to be, and that there is a first thing he could say to anybody who asked him and that he is not going to say it, and that he has said that out loud so that everybody in the room knows there is one and nobody can say afterwards that he led anybody up to it — and the man of about thirty-four said it back to him, and neither of them thanked the other, and no chapter of these ten says what the first thing is and no chapter asks him.** He is right about nothing in these ten chapters and is not defeated in any of them.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF. A tally-board, a knife, and a barrow, and a space at the top of his boards about the length of a barrow where nothing is.** He is in Chapters 0412 and 0414. **In Chapter 0412 he worked the boards on a Sunday and said out loud, plainly, that he gave the barrow away in about a quarter of an hour on the Sunday it went, that he did not say why and is not going to say why now, and that nobody in this city has asked him about it once and has not asked him since and is not going to; and nobody on those boards told him he was right and nobody asked him why.** In Chapter 0414 he came up the nine steps with the tally-board under his arm and the knife in his hand for a reason nobody has worked out, turned the tally-board so that its face was away from the room, and **gave one figure to the room — about four hundred and forty, on eleven miles of flats — and nobody asked him a second question about it, and the figure he has is printed nowhere in these ten and the difference between the two figures is stated nowhere in these ten.** **He is not the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, that man is in none of these ten chapters, and the two were not merged.** Neither of his two acts in the last volume is repeated, restated, improved on or put in one room on one day in either of his two chapters, and nobody in these ten asks him what a column is for and no chapter answers it.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT. A document inside the coat, and no local language.** He is in Chapters 0415 and 0419 and **is in neither the room where anything got decided nor a description of one.** He said out loud in Chapter 0415, for the second time in this city and without being asked, that he does not have the local language, and said it a second time rather than once because nobody had said it for him, **and nobody in either chapter arranged for the language, nobody offered to find somebody who had it, and it is not mended in this volume.** He took a document out of the front of the coat, laid it across his own knee, kept a palm flat on the whole of it, read it for about as long as it takes to count nine, shut it, put it back and buttoned the coat. **He did not describe the room and he did not tell anybody where he had been and he has not been in a room in this city in which anything got decided and is not going to be.** **In Chapter 0419 it was worked out in front of him, out loud, at the back of the room, that nobody in that room has read one word of the page his office sent, and nobody said it to him, nobody did anything about it, and he is not exposed and not converted and not put down.** **The nine sentences about a return that are his are not said, restated, paraphrased or improved on in either chapter, and neither chapter prints one of them.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FOUR — VOLUME 09'S ONE NEW PERSON. A length of blue thread tied round her own wrist, which is how she has always known a length by.** She is on a page once, in Chapter 0420, and in no other of the ten, and she is in no chapter of this volume after this one. `woman of about fifty-four` stands at **four** occurrences in Chapter 0420 and at **zero** in the other nine files of this batch, and the descriptor is the one `outline/volume-09.md` §7.2 took from the forty-seven, at three occurrences in one file across four hundred chapter files and at zero in Volumes 07 and 08. **She was put on a page by being asked: the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asked her, standing up, in the local language, in daylight, with a date on the door, where her line is, and she answered it correctly.** What she said is that her line is where the water stops on her own step, and that it is at the second board, and that it is not the same line twice in the same week, and that she knows a length by a thread and there is one of them round her wrist for it, and that she has never once put that line on a page and did not come up those nine steps to have it put on one. **Nobody asked her a second question, nobody in that room asked her where her step is, how long it is, who is on the other side of it, or how she came to know a length by a thread, nobody said thank you, and she was thanked by nobody in any of the ten.** Her line is one of the four and **nothing was written on any page in the world when she gave it, which the chapter says out loud in the register-keeper's mouth, and the line is on no page at all.** **Her line is the one that comes back moved on day 440, and no chapter of this batch traces a move, investigates one, asks who made one, reports one to a room, guesses at one, or says in any mouth that anybody moved anything, and nothing has moved because nothing has been drawn.** She is not given a trade, a board, a market, a house or a name, and nothing in these ten merges her with anybody.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN, A BUCKET AND A RAG.** He is in Chapter 0417 and in no other of the ten. **He washed the store's board through a squall that had been washing the wall above it for about a quarter of an hour while he stood with his arm over his face and a bucket in his other hand, and he had decided about a month ago not to wash the wall and the sky washed it for him; the man of about thirty-four came out of the weather and saved the board, and the man the board belongs to said out loud that he did not stand there and ask, and then said out loud that he was not going to let it get about that a man washed a board he did not have to wash.** The board carries the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day and still does, and it has not been written on since the two hundred and twenty-fifth, and nobody in that market thanked either of them.

**AND THE ONES WHO ARE NOT IN THIS BATCH AND WHO WERE NOT ASKED ABOUT ANYTHING IN IT.** The man of about thirty-nine, who spent the four-second beat in Chapter 0406 and is thanked in none of these ten and is in none of them. The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living, who is in no chapter of these ten and did not open anything. Tamsin Quill, who is in no chapter of these ten. The man of about fifty-nine, the man of about sixty and his handcart, the man of about thirty-three who keeps this room, the man of about thirty-seven, the man of about fifty-two who digs, the man of about sixty-three who farms the half of the holding, the man of about sixty-four who digs somebody else's ditch: all in no chapter of this batch and none of them asked anything and none of them sent for. The woman of about twenty-one with the pen with the nib ground narrow is on a road and was not asked about anything. The fourth of the four is not asked about anything. The woman of about sixty-nine, the woman of about fifty at a shut gate, the girl of seventeen, the keeper of the Redroot gate, the woman of about forty-eight in a fourth house at Nine Elms, the Regent, the man of about thirty-two on the bank and the man of about thirty-one on the bank are all in no chapter of this batch and were not sent for.

**AND THE DESCRIPTORS THIS BATCH DID NOT USE, published because a batch that keeps a list of what it avoided is doing the thing a record is for.** *Man of about thirty-seven* is at zero across the ten. *Man of about forty-four* is at zero. *Woman of about thirty-four* is at zero, **so the co-presence prohibition is held at zero on the word-bounded reading and at zero on the unbounded reading and the narration-line relaxation at `outline/volume-09.md` §20.9 was not needed.** *About two hundred rooms* is at zero, and **no room count at all is printed in any of the ten, and two were cut out of this batch's first pass and the repair is published at the batch record §11.5.** *Man of about thirty-one on the bank* and *man of about thirty-two on the bank* are at zero. **The man of about thirty-three who keeps a room over a market is not in this batch and is not a new man, and what this batch uses is the room, and the room is a room and not a man, and the man who keeps it stays off the page.**

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0002 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — the cast as it now stands, and this block wins over every block above it

**A review of this batch repaired four of its ten pages and found three faults in its own record, and **exactly two of those faults are about a person**, and both are corrected here. No descriptor was changed, given a second figure or taken away, no new person was put on a page, and the volume's list of nine chapters Adrian Vale is in is untouched at 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447. Volume 09's one new person is still on a page once, in Chapter 0420, and this pass did not put her in a second chapter and did not ask her anything.**

**1. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE IS IN CHAPTER 0412 AND THE BLOCK ABOVE SAYS HE IS NOT IN THIS BATCH, AND THAT SENTENCE IS WRONG, and the error was in this file and not on a page.** He is in Chapter 0412 three times and in no other of the ten. **He came down onto the boards at about the eighth hour with his own board under his arm, in the way he has stood at the bottom of things in this city for about a fortnight, and he stood there for the whole of the morning and did not go up, and he said four things, and not one of the four was about the sheet on a table in a room over a market.** The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf told him, unasked and in his own mouth, that he gave a barrow away in about a quarter of an hour on the Sunday it went, that he did not say why and is not going to say why now, and that nobody in this city has asked him about it once; and he was told that a sheet of paper with a place for a person in it is not a barrow and does not need one. **Nobody on those boards told him he was right and nobody asked him why.** He is a party of four and he does not go and stand in one room over a market saying anything, and the two of them said nine sentences between them on a set of boards and none of the nine was about the thing that room is about.

**2. THE ONE THING THAT CHANGED ABOUT HIM AT THIS REPAIR PASS, and it is a thing he did on a page and not a thing anybody said about him.** He laid the board he had come down with flat on the wharf at the bottom end and squared one end of it with his heel, and the man of about thirty-eight turned his tally-board face down onto the boards, put his thumb about a third of the way along it and ran the knife down it once, and one length came off it and went over on its side on the wet boards. **It came off about a forearm long because nobody told him how long to cut it, and he said so out loud — that he did not tell him how long and that he was not asked — and the man of about thirty-eight answered in the shortest sentence he says in the whole chapter, that he has got it too long and will have to saw it himself, and neither of them said thank you and neither of them was thanked.** He looked at the length lying on the boards before he picked it up, and he went up off the wharf with it under his arm on top of the board he had come down with. **He is thanked in no chapter of the ten, he is asked what a line is in no chapter of the ten, and nothing in this repair pass asks him anything or puts him in a chapter he is not in.**

**3. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF HAS NOW GIVEN AWAY TWO THINGS IN ONE VOLUME, and no page joins them.** The barrow went on day 391, a Sunday, and the length of board came off the wharf on day 412, a Sunday, and **no chapter says the two are the same kind of thing, no chapter says the two are the same decision, and no chapter says why either of them happened.** He still says the reason is not one he is going to give. **He is thanked in none of the three chapters he is in and is asked nothing in any of them, and the second thing is not a gift that anybody thanked him for and not a thing the man who received it made a speech about.** The strip of clear board at the bottom of the same stack is a different piece of wood, it did not move, it is a piece of board and not a page, and it is on nobody's list of anything.

**4. THE THIRD FAULT IN THE RECORD WAS NOT ABOUT A PERSON AND IS PUBLISHED HERE ANYWAY, because a cast record is where a marker count belongs when the count is a count of a word people use about their own bodies.** The marker *mark* stands at **five** across the ten and the batch's own record published **four**, and the fifth is **a length of blue thread worn into the wrist of the woman of about fifty-four**, in the narration that says she has been measuring a thing by hand for long enough that the measuring has worn a mark. **The other four are the ordinary word about the crease pressed down the third of the four places on the sheet — twice in Chapter 0413, once in Chapter 0416 in a mouth, and once in Chapter 0420's coda — and none of the five is about anything in a field and the word *field* stands at zero across the ten.** Her own record in the block above is not changed by this: she is on a page once, she was asked where her line is and answered it correctly, nobody asked her a second question, nobody thanked her, the thread is her second handle and it is on no page before Chapter 0420, and she is in no chapter of this volume after that one.

**5. WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO TO ANY PERSON.** No descriptor was given a second figure, no age was printed anywhere, **and the one instance the review noted and did not call a fault stands as a note: Chapter 0412 gives one of the three men on the road a fresh age, of about twenty-five, in the sentence that says a man came up out of the low ground and took the barrow off the top of the boards, and he is aged nowhere else in the volume.** He is canon, the page that carries him is at `bible/power-system.md` and Chapter 0327, and the age is in his own arrival and is not a second figure for him. **The man of about fifty-seven is in four of the ten and is asked nothing in any of them, his rope is still over the back of his chair, and this pass did not touch him and did not put him in a chapter he is not in. The man of about forty-one in a very good coat is in Chapters 0415 and 0419 and was not put down, converted or exposed, and the language was not arranged for him. The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register asked her one question in Chapter 0420 and is thanked by nobody in any of the four chapters she speaks in. Adrian Vale is in Chapter 0414 and in no other of the ten and reads a page in it, and nothing here warms up Chapter 0436.**
