# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0003 — the cast as it stands after days 421 to 430, and this block wins over every block above it

**A batch has run and wrote ten chapters. Everything below is on a page in `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0421.md` through `chapter-0430.md` or is published in `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0003.md` with the instrument that produced it, and no descriptor below was changed, given a second figure, or taken away. Volume 09's one new person is on a page once, in Chapter 0420, is in none of these ten, and was not put in a second chapter and was not asked a second question. No second person was added in this batch and the ten rooms it wrote are rooms about nine people were already in.**

**AND THE GAP IN THIS BATCH'S BRIEFING, because a cast record that hides where its cards were not is a cast record that is wrong about itself: the prompt told this batch to look for its own card file at `outline/batches/volume-09-batch-0003.md` before reading it and that file does not exist on disk.** The cast below was reconstructed from `outline/volume-09.md` §7, §7.2, §7.3, §8, §9, §15, §16, §17 and §20.6 and from the ten chapters before it. The next batch is told to look for its own card file before it reads it and to say so in its record if it is not there, and not to create one.

**ADRIAN VALE, STAGE 2, A WITNESS, IN THE DOING, ALL TEN DAYS.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once across the ten files, nobody offers him a workway, the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the ten, and he is aged nowhere in them. **He is named in Chapter 0422 and in Chapter 0428, twice in each, and in no chapter in any form in the other eight.** In Chapter 0422 he came up the nine steps at about the twelfth hour, was not asked why he was in the room and was not asked for anything while he was in it, **took a sheet of his own out of the inside of his coat, a date on the back of it and two names on the front of it and no third, carried it to the window end of the table and read it standing up without sitting down and without opening it out and without putting it down flat, and then folded it the way it had been, put it back and buttoned the coat; and the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall was at the back of that room, had not been asked anything that morning, did not ask anything, and watched the whole of it.** In Chapter 0428 he had his hand on the sheet on the table, **read the first of the four places and the second of them twice and the fourth, and did not read the third, and the one place on that page he did not read that morning is the one with a person in it.** **He said not one word about what the man at the back of the room said, he did not tell him what he had said, he did not tell anybody it was the name of anything, nobody in that room asked him anything about it, and nobody in that room thanked him for being in the room.** He is in neither Chapter 0425 nor any chapter in any form in it, **he is not in Chapter 0436, which is not this batch's, and nothing in these ten warms it up, and the volume's list of nine is untouched at 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER. Her second slate.** She is in eight of the ten and speaks in six of them and is thanked by nobody in any of them and no chapter says she was right. **In Chapter 0421 she stood up and put her finger on the paper beside the third of the four places and not in it, and asked a room to say the rest of a thing, and later that morning said out loud that she has told that room every morning since the Sunday it was read out that she is not the one who is going to decide, and that she has said it so many mornings that it has gone flat, and that she is going to stop saying it on Saturday morning and that there is no morning after Saturday to say it in.** In Chapter 0422 **she carried the sheet out onto the step at the back in her own two hands, still folded the way it came, and stood beside it, and told a woman in the market, out loud, in the local language, that the third of the four is a person and that she does not know what it is for and is not going to start on a step in a market.** In Chapter 0424 **she asked the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall what he sells and then what he does with the rest of his day, standing up, in the local language, in daylight, with a date on the door, and neither question was about the table, and she wrote nothing down because there is nowhere on that table to write down what a man sells.** On day 425 **she decided out loud that the third of the four places was going to be left empty; she said what the third place is first, in nine words, in her own mouth; she said the cost out loud before the decision and the reason for the cost is hers; there was no vote and nobody asked for one; and she is thanked by nobody.** In Chapter 0428 **she asked him what a line is, once, standing up, in the local language, in daylight, with a date on the door, in front of about nine people, and gave her reason for the asking once afterwards, in her own mouth, and nobody in that room improved on it and nobody asked her for it twice.** **Her second slate is named in two of the ten chapters, in Chapter 0425 and in Chapter 0430, it carries a date and nothing under it in both, and in both it was not picked up and was not turned over and was not written on, and in Chapter 0430 a woman in that room is worked out, in narration, to be the one person who could have written a second one and that a second one is not going to be written either and nobody asks her for one.** Her book's third column has been empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days and no line was entered in that book in any of the ten. She is not Tamsin Quill, the two of them are never in one room in these ten chapters, and nothing here settles whether they are one woman.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL. A piece of chalk he does not use, in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day two hundred and ninety-eight.** He is present in eight of the ten and speaks in seven, is asked two questions in all of them, and **is thanked in none of the seven and argued with in none of the seven.** **The full form of the day the chalk went into that pocket is printed at full length in five of the ten chapters, in a mouth in one of them and in narration in four, and no chapter of the ten shortens it to a relative phrase and there is no *the last week of that month* in any of them.** What he says in the ten: in Chapter 0421 that a man at the end of a bench is not being spared and is not being reached and that those are two different things; in Chapter 0422 that a woman has just told a stranger in a market that she does not know and that he is not going to be the one who tells the room she is wrong; in Chapter 0424 two answers about his trade and his day, neither of them about the page; in Chapter 0425 that there is nothing for him to do in that room and has not been for a fortnight, and that he has a piece of chalk in that pocket and it is going to stay in that pocket; in Chapter 0428 five words in the local language in answer to being asked what a line is, said once; in Chapter 0429 that he has not been asked which one of the four is his and is not going to be, and that he wrote a line on a wall a long way up a road once, last, on purpose, and has not said whether it works and is not going to say today either, and that neither of those two things is the one that was said to him. **The ninth line is not moved, not dated, not read aloud, not taken down, not thanked for and not resolved in any of the ten, and whether it works stays unanswered, and Chapter 0429's coda says in its own words that the ninth line and the four lines on a page are not the same thing, and no chapter of these ten has him say he knows what he said.** **Nobody in this flood has ever asked him what a line is except the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register, on day 428, and nothing in the other nine chapters warms it up.** Two costs that are his and not the volume's: **his stall stood shut for the whole of Saturday day 425 because he was up nine steps in the room, and about nine people who came to that market found a shuttered board, and a voice at the bottom of the stair on day 430 told him he had lost two mornings and he did not get up and did not go down and was not thanked for opening up at all.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN. A length of new rope he has never used, over the back of a chair.** He is in five of the ten — Chapters 0421, 0423, 0424, 0425 and 0430 — **is asked nothing in any of them, is not told anything in any of them, does not leave, and is not happy.** **Nobody asks him where the fence is, nobody tells him, he does not move the fence, and he does not touch the rope; the rope is over the back of a chair in four of the five and is in a room in this city in all five, and in the one chapter of the ten that is set on that road, Chapter 0423, the rope is not named at all and he is on the low side of a fence with nothing in his hand and nobody offers him a hand and he does not offer one.** **In Chapter 0423 he said out loud, unasked, to nobody, on a shut road, that both a room that does not ask him to hold anything and a man doing a thing two feet from his hands who does not ask him are the same arrangement, and that he is not going to stand in the wet and decide on a shut road which one of them it is.** That is a statement that he is not going to say which, and it is not a hint at anything, and no chapter of these ten says what either thing is or asks him anything at all. He is right about nothing in these ten chapters and is not defeated in any of them and has not tried to move the fence. **In Chapter 0421, the day a room worked out out loud that it had no instrument for asking him anything, nobody in that room looked at him while it was being said.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF. A tally-board, a knife with the edge gone blunt on it, and a space at the top of his boards about the length of a barrow where nothing is.** He is in Chapter 0427 and in no other of the ten. **He came up the nine steps before the light was off the low ground, with the tally-board under his arm and the knife in his hand, and put the board on a table in a room with a date on the door with the face of it up, which he has never done in this flood, and did not read from it, and nobody in that room asked him what was on it.** He said out loud why he has been turning it over — **that it is not modesty, it is that a figure he has said out loud is a figure somebody is going to ask him where he got — and that the one he sends is not the one he has, and that there is no page in this city that has both of them on it, and that he has stopped turning it over. He printed neither figure and the difference between them is stated with no number attached to either.** **Nobody in that room asked him for the second figure and nobody asked him where the first one came from, and he put his hand flat on the face of the board and left it there and said nothing about it, and he was thanked by nobody.** He went down the nine steps at about the third hour with the board under his arm and the knife in his hand and the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall was coming up them at the same time, and **they passed each other on the stair and neither of them said one word, and no chapter of these ten merges him with the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, and that man is in none of the ten.** Neither of his two acts in Volume 08 is repeated, restated, improved on or put in one room on one day, and nobody in these ten asks him what a column is for.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD. A board.** He is in seven of the ten, speaks in five, is thanked in none of the five, and **on the first of the ten days he said out loud, in a room, that four of them had been in it every working morning since the page came up the road and not one of them had gone down nine steps and put one question to one man, and gave the reason, which is that a sheet goes out of this city four times a year and comes back with things in it that were not written on it, and that there is no form in four hundred miles that puts a question to a man.** In Chapter 0422 he came up the nine steps with his own board under his arm and a piece of bark in his hand and put the bark on the table and left it there, and said out loud how many people had been at the market end of that step and that nobody else in the room was standing where he was standing. **On day 425 a fair practical question was put to him at the point where a room ran out of forms, and he said nothing at all, his right hand stayed flat on the face of his own slate, and he was not asked it again.** In Chapter 0430 he said out loud that the page is the only thing anybody in this city owns that gets looked at every morning and has nothing in it, and that he does not know what to do with having noticed it. **The four-second beat is not spent in any of the ten and the exact string is at zero, and the board nobody has written on is not mentioned in any of them, and the man who traded on the Open Hand board is not given a trade, a board or a line he did not have.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE ARE THE ONLY TWO MEN IN CHAPTER 0424 AND THE ONLY TWO IN THAT ROOM WHO HAVE BEEN ASKED NOTHING ALL MONTH, and the chapter says so in its own words and puts the two men who have been asked nothing either side of each other on a bench with one sentence between them and no more.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, A HAZEL MALLET WITH ITS HEAD BOUND IN CORD AND A COIL OF ROPE ON TOP OF THE COIL.** He is in Chapter 0423 and in no other of the ten, and **his descriptor is a bare tens word and that is a deviation from `outline/volume-09.md` §17 item 15 and it is published in the batch record §3 item 9, and the reason is Batch 0001's: he is a returning person whose descriptor is on a page at `chapters/volume-09/chapter-0410.md:15` and the collision the rule exists to prevent is a reader reading a number fast.** He untied a withie whose binding had gone, cut a new length of cord off the coil with his own knife, and put the withie back on the post it belongs to at the height it was at. **He said nothing at all about where the fence is, nobody asked him, he did not agree with the man at the end of that fence about anything, he did not ask that man a single question, and nothing was said to him about being at the end of that fence.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN, A BUCKET AND A RAG.** He is in Chapter 0426 and in no other of the ten. **He came past a stall on a Sunday morning off his own Saturday, stopped for the length of about a sentence, said what the arrangement of the two of them has been since before the water, and neither of them put a bucket down or picked up a piece of string, and he went on past and said nothing about it afterwards.** Nobody in that market thanked either of them. The store's board is not named in that chapter and the wall above it is not named in it and the chapter does not repeat Chapter 0417.

# VOLUME 09, BATCH 0003 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS — and this block wins over every block above it

**NOT ONE PERSON MOVED, no age, no descriptor, no trade, no second handle, no second handle removed, no person invented and no person removed, and the review repair of Batch 0003 edited three of the ten chapter files and changed three sentences in them and every one of those sentences is about a fact already on a page and not about a person.** The full account is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0003.md` §14.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF, AND THE ONE PLACE A SENTENCE ABOUT HIM WAS WRONG.** He is in Chapter 0427 and in no other of the ten, and the chapter's narration has been corrected in one clause. It said he had been putting one number into a column four times a year for nine years without anybody ever asking him what the column was for. **He was asked what the column was for, twice, on day 357: once by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register standing on the wet boards, at `chapters/volume-08/chapter-0357.md:15`, and once in a room with the day on the door at the fourth hour. He gave no answer on the boards and gave one in the room, in seven words. What nobody has ever asked him is which of the two numbers he puts in it, and `chapter-0357.md:39` says exactly that and is careful to say it.** The sentence on the page now carries both facts, and **nothing else about him moved: he came up the nine steps before the light was off the low ground, he put the tally-board on his knees with the face of it up, he did not read from it, he said out loud why he has been turning it over and that he has stopped, and he printed neither figure.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL, AND THE TWO DAYS A VOICE AT THE BOTTOM OF A STAIR NAMED.** He is present in eight of the ten, speaks in seven, is asked two questions in all of them and is thanked in none. **Chapter 0430's line now says a Monday and a Tuesday where it said a Tuesday and a Sunday, and the reason is on two pages and not on one: the Sunday in the first version was day 426 and `chapter-0426.md:3` sets that whole morning in the market with his board on its string and him behind it and the man of about twenty-seven stopping to talk.** Days 427 and 428 are named by no page as shut and by no page as open. **What he does about it did not move and is what the chapter is: he did not get up, he did not say one word back down the stair, and at the fourth hour he went all the way down and put his own board on its string and the cord coiled beside it, and nobody in that market knew and he was not thanked for opening up at all.** **His chalk is in his pocket in all ten and was not taken out in any of them and nobody asked him for it in any of them.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER, AND THE DEADLINE SHE SET ON HERSELF.** She is in eight of the ten and speaks in six and is thanked by nobody. **Chapter 0421's sentence in which she said she had said it so many mornings that it had gone flat and was going to stop saying it on Saturday morning no longer says that there is no morning after Saturday to say it in, and it now says that on the Saturday she is going to say the other thing instead, which is what Chapter 0425 has and which keeps the day she set and the decision she took on it exactly as the batch wrote them.** She decided the third of the four places empty on day 425 with the cost named first, in nine words, with no vote, and she is thanked by nobody, and no chapter of this volume gives a reader a reason.

**THE OTHERS, UNMOVED.** Adrian Vale is in Chapters 0422 and 0428 and in no other of the ten, and in both he is in a room reading a page and is asked for nothing. The man of about fifty-seven is in five of the ten and is asked nothing in any of them. The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board is in seven and speaks in five and is thanked in none of the five. The man of about sixty with the hazel mallet is in Chapter 0423 only, and the man of about twenty-seven with a bucket and a rag is in Chapter 0426 only. **The woman of about fifty-four is still in none of these ten chapters and the descriptor is still at zero across the ten files, and the man of about fifty-two is still in none of them, and the man of about thirty-seven, the man of about forty-four and the woman of about thirty-four are still at zero.**

## Volume 09, Batch 0004 — Chapters 0431–0440, days 431 to 440 (written 2026)

**Days 431 to 440, one chapter to one day, no double day, the Bare Month days 116 to 125. This block wins over everything above it in this file, and it does not touch line 223 above, which is still line 223 because an append goes at the foot.**

- **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** is in nine of the ten chapters and speaks in four, and is thanked by nobody in any of the four. She said out loud that four lines are going to have to be drawn by four of the nine who were in that room on the Sunday the page was read out, because those are the only nine anybody in this city is able to name; she asked the only question Chapter 0432 asked; **she said out loud that she is the one who is going to put the page into a carrier's hand and that the woman who leaves a page like that empty of names is the one person in this city it can be said to have come from, and that it is not the same as sending it and will be the same as sending it by the time it has been on a cart for a week; she refused out loud to be handed to anybody and to hand it to anybody either; she folded the sheet along the two folds that were already in it, put a stone off a doorstep on it, and gave it to a man who carries things for a living; and on the Sunday it came back she told a man to stop looking at the page because there is no page in this city she can put it on.** Her second slate is named in three of the ten and named as where it lies in a fourth, and in all four it carries a date and nothing under the date and nobody went near the shelf. **Her book's third column is empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days and no line was entered in it, and the line entered on day 443 is a later chapter's.** She is not Tamsin Quill, the two of them are never in one room in these ten, and nothing here settles whether they are one woman.
- **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall** is in six of the ten and speaks in five and is thanked in none of the five, and he has asked in advance, in a lane, not to be thanked. He said that a man who has not been asked puts down whatever his hand is on; that he said before a mark was on the paper that he is not one of the four; that he carried a board up nine steps and put it against a wall and is not asking the room for anything else; that he will go down those steps first on the Friday; and **on day 438 he refused this volume's one rule out loud in about four seconds and said what it costs, which is his own line on a wall nine miles up that road, and that the reason nothing has moved it is that there has been nobody on that road to move it.** His chalk is still in the inside breast pocket and was not taken out on any of the ten days. **The ninth line is named once, is not moved, is not dated, is not read aloud, is not taken down and is not resolved, and it is not the same thing as the four marks on the page and no chapter of these ten says it is, and no chapter of these ten has him say he knows what he said on day 428.**
- **The man of about fifty-seven** is in three of the ten — Chapters 0432, 0435 and 0439 — and is asked nothing in any of them, and is not told where the fence is, and does not move it, and does not touch the rope. **He is one of the four who drew a mark in the second of the four places and he read that mark out loud in the local language because it was in front of him and the room had stopped talking, and he did not say it was his, and nobody in that room suggested it and nobody asked him.** He said two things to his barrow on a shut road, one of them that nobody ever asks him to hold anything, and the man of about sixty answered him once and did not stop working and did not ask him one question. He is right about nothing in these ten and is not defeated in any of them. **The length of new rope has still never been used, and the coil in the handcart is a different rope, and the chapter that names both of them joins them in nothing.**
- **The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board** is in seven of the ten and speaks in six and is thanked in none of the six. He asked, on the day before the page went out, the volume's practical question, which was who was going to take it out of the room, and it was a fair question and nobody had asked it before. He drew one of the four marks and said nothing at all while he drew it. He spent the middle of the Saturday after saying that he had nothing to say about a clean rectangle on a table. **On day 438 he was right in about four seconds and the chapter does not say so and nobody in that room told him he was right or that he was wrong, and the room let him have the whole of it and said nothing.** On day 440 he is the only person in the batch on the page about the page, and what he says there is about the paper and not about the four.
- **The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf** is in Chapter 0436 and in no other of the ten, and **no chapter of this batch merges him with the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, who is in none of them.** He worked the boards, did not lift his head when a woman came down onto them, and said one practical thing to her and one practical thing to a man carrying a length of board past. **A stone at the lower end of those boards came off a building when the water came and cannot be moved, and nobody tried to move it, nobody offered to help move it, nobody in that chapter asked anybody anything about it, and the clear strip of board at the bottom of the stack is a piece of wood and is on nobody's list of anything.** He is thanked in no chapter of the ten.
- **The man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living** is in Chapters 0438 and 0440 and in no other of the ten. He carried the sheet out of this city in a satchel with a flap on day 438 and did not open it, and brought the same kind of satchel up nine steps on day 440 and put it on a table and did not open it, and said nothing about where he had had it or how long he had had it or who had handed it to him, and nobody asked him. **That is the last thing that has gone past him in this flood and the plan's own count of what he has carried stands at seven and is not changed by these two chapters, and no sheet of paper with a day on it and a place on it and nothing at the top of it has gone past him or is going to.**
- **The man of about fifty-nine** went up the road out of this city with his barrow on day 439 and came back down it on day 440, and the page came up the road on the same Sunday, **and the two are separate events and neither of them had anything to do with the other and no chapter of these ten prints a count of how far he has got.** He is not going after the three who went down the four-hundred-mile road and is not asking about them.
- **The woman of about fifty-two** drew one of the four marks, said out loud, unasked, that she had known before she came up the steps that she was going to put a mark on that page and that she had said so in that room before and that nobody has asked her since how she knew, and was thanked by nobody. **Her own door is not knocked on in any of these ten.**
- **The man of about thirty-six** drew one of the four marks, said one thing that was not about the page on his way out, and was not in that room again that morning. **The man of about sixty with the hazel mallet** packed about nine yards of soft cart-track on the low side of a shut road, was not doing anything to the fence, did not offer the man of about fifty-seven the end of a wedge, and is a bare tens word and the deviation is published. **The man of about twenty-seven** carried a length of board past the lower end of a wharf and took it round and nobody thanked him.
- **Tamsin Quill** came down onto the boards of the salt wharf on the Wednesday that is the ninth and last of the nine chapters Adrian Vale is in, stood at the bottom of them for about an hour with a folded sheet of road in the inside of her coat and about nine places on it, did not take it out, said one thing to the man working them, and was not asked a question by anybody on that wharf.
- **Nobody else moved.** **The woman of about fifty-four is in none of these ten chapters and the descriptors `woman of about fifty-four` and `blue thread` both stand at zero across all ten files, and she is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and the reason is not that she would not answer.** The man of about forty-one in a very good coat is in none of them and the phrase for the distance of his office stands at zero. The man of about fifty-nine is in two of them and only in narration. The woman of about sixty-nine is in none of them. **The woman of about twenty-one is on a road and was not asked about anything, and the days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of these ten.** Nobody got stronger, nothing was resolved, and the third of the four places is empty and the third column is empty and one of the four marks came back in a place it had not gone out in.

## Volume 09, Batch 0004 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS, and this block wins over every block above it

**The people in Batch 0004's ten chapters are the same people they were. A repair pass may not change a cast member, and this one did not, and the review asked it to and the answer is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-09-batch-0004.md` §12.6: a name is a canon change, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall has been tracked by that handle through eight volumes and four hundred and thirty chapters, and putting a name on him on day 431 would put a fact on a page that contradicts every page before it.**

**WHAT MOVED IN THE PROSE THESE PEOPLE SPEAK, and it is three mouths and no mouth.** The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall, in Chapter 0434, no longer claims three achievements where two of them were one silence counted twice; **his three are now three different acts, and the number, the self-accounting and the refusal to be thanked are all still his, and nothing he said in Chapter 0428 or Chapter 0428's answer is touched and neither is quoted here.** The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board is untouched in every mouth he has across the ten, including the one in Chapter 0440 in which he says he cannot tell which of the four marks moved and why, and the one in Chapter 0438 in which he is right about a rule and is told he is neither right nor wrong. The man of about fifty-seven is untouched: he reads a mark out loud in Chapter 0432 without saying it was his, and nothing in the repair changes that or the fact that nobody in that room can afterwards say which of the four is his. **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall's refusal in Chapter 0438 is not printed in this file and was not read into it, and neither is the volume's one rule, and neither is anything the man of about thirty-eight said in Chapter 0357 or Chapter 0393.**

**WHAT ELSE IS THE SAME.** Adrian Vale is in Chapters 0432 and 0436 and in no other of the ten, named in both, reads a page in one and reads nothing in the other, performed no working, opened no threshold, was offered no workway and asked for none, and the ninth and last of the nine chapters this volume has him in is still 0436. **Tamsin Quill is on the wharf in Chapter 0436 with a folded sheet of road in the inside of her coat and about nine places on it and does not take it out, and the two objects are still never laid beside one another.** The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register is still the one who hands the page to the carrier on day 438 and still the one who opens the satchel on day 440, and nobody is ever going to ask her about the mark that moved. The woman of about fifty-two still told that room a fortnight before day 432 that one of the four lines was going to be hers. The man of about thirty-four's piece of chalk is still in his inside breast pocket and has not been asked for in about a dozen rooms and has not been given to anybody. The man of about fifty-seven's length of new rope is still over the back of his chair and is still unused, and the coil of rope in a handcart on a shut road is still a different rope and is still in the same chapter without being joined to anything.

**THE ONE FIGURE CORRECTED** is a house figure and not a person: the long sentences of forty words or more are **a hundred and twelve on both named readings**, and the hundred and eight and the hundred and six that two files published for the second reading are withdrawn and both are named at the figure. ## Volume 09, Batch 0005 — Chapters 0441–0450, days 441 to 450 (written 2026)

**Days 441 to 450, one chapter to one day, no double day, the Bare Month days are 126 to 135, and 135 is the last day of that month any chapter of this volume may write. THIS IS THE LAST BATCH OF VOLUME 09 AND THIS BLOCK WINS OVER EVERY BLOCK ABOVE IT IN THIS FILE. THIS BLOCK DOES NOT TOUCH LINE 223 ABOVE, WHICH IS STILL LINE 223, BECAUSE AN APPEND GOES AT THE FOOT AND THE POINTER IS UNAFFECTED BY IT. THERE IS NO BATCH 0006 OF THIS VOLUME AND THIS BATCH CREATED NO NEXT PHASE OF ANY KIND.**

- **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** is in six of the ten chapters — 0441, 0443, 0444, 0445, 0447 and 0450 — and speaks in four of them and is thanked by nobody in any of the four. She said out loud that there is not one page in four hundred miles that tells a city when its own cart is going out, that she had been asking four people who sell things, and that she is not going to ask a second time. **On the day the register got its second line she said, before the pen went near the page, what a book with a day in it and nothing under the day is for and that it is the only thing a man four hundred miles off cannot ask this city to produce, and then that nothing goes in the second column and no name goes in the third, and the chapter says in its own words that she ruled the line herself, wrote the day, and read it back before she wrote anything else.** She said out loud that she is not going to fold the page again until it is going out again and that it is not going to be folded by a person who has not been asked. On the last of the ten days she put her hand flat on the table beside the page, did not touch it, and said the third of those four places is empty on purpose and nobody in the room is going to put anything in it. **Her second slate is named in seven of the ten and in all seven it carries a date and nothing under the date and nobody went near the shelf. Her book's third column is empty on purpose and is empty on purpose after these ten days, and the only line this batch entered in that book is the second one.**
- **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall** is in seven of the ten — 0441, 0444, 0445, 0446, 0447, 0449 and 0450 — and speaks in four and is thanked in none of the four, and he asked in advance, in a lane a long way back, not to be thanked. He said out loud that the day the cart comes is not the thing and gave his own reason, which is what a page does rather than when it goes. He said out loud, with a woman standing at the end of the bench, that there is a draft on that bench every morning of this flood and that he would like nobody in the room to make anything of it. He answered a woman asking about a cold with the reason he does not tell her, which is that if he told her she would stop saying it out loud and that she is the only person in that room who says it. He said out loud that the page does not get folded and he does not get asked, and that those are two things and he is not going to say either of them again. **His chalk is still in the inside breast pocket and was not taken out on any of the ten days, and the ninth line is not named in any of these ten chapters and is not the same thing as the four marks on the page and no chapter of these ten says it is, and no chapter of these ten has him say he knows what he said on day 428.**
- **The man of about fifty-seven** is in five of the ten — 0442, 0443, 0444, 0445 and 0449 — and is asked nothing in any of them, and is not told where the fence is, and does not move it, and does not touch the rope. He said one thing to his barrow on a shut road and the man of about sixty answered him once without stopping work. **In Chapter 0449 the length of new rope came off the back of his chair because a woman moved that chair to let somebody past her, and he bent down and put it back where it had been, and nobody in that room said one word about it and nobody asked him what it is for.** He is right about nothing in these ten and is not defeated in any of them. **The length of new rope has still never been used, and the coil in the handcart is a different rope, and the chapter that names both of them joins them in nothing.**
- **The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board** is in seven of the ten — 0441, 0443, 0444, 0446, 0447, 0449 and 0450 — and speaks in seven and is thanked in none of the seven. He asked the practical question about the cart, gave the other half of his own answer to the table, said the plain question in the room where the register got its second line, **was wrong about four stalls in a market and was told he was wrong in front of about nine people and was not told he was right and did not argue and did not concede**, said out loud that he would rather have been wrong about a market than right about a page and that he was not going to say the other thing because nobody in that room would be able to prove it, **stopped at a stall front for the second time in this flood and bought cord there with no measure said out loud in either direction**, said the plain thing in the room where a page is waiting for a round, said one thing about a day of the week arriving earlier than it does on the others, and on the last of the ten days had his slate on his knee and said nothing at all.
- **The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf is in none of these ten chapters, and Tamsin Quill is in none of them, and no chapter of this batch merges him with the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347, who is also in none of them.** The strip of clear board, the stone and the figure on the flats are in none of these ten and no figure about the flats is printed in any of them.
- **The man of about sixty with the hazel mallet** is in two of the ten — Chapters 0442 and 0448 — packed about nine yards of cart-track on the low side of a shut road and then on the high side of it, was not doing anything to the fence, did not offer the man of about fifty-seven the end of a wedge, and was answered once. **He is a bare tens word and the deviation is published, and it is the same deviation Batch 0001 published and that five earlier chapters of this volume carry, and this batch did not resolve it and did not reroute it.**
- **The man of about fifty-nine** went up a shut road out of this city with a hurdle of hazel rods in the bottom of the barrow that he made and a bar across the top of it that he replaced, and came back down it, and stopped on the low side of that fence and picked it up again. **No chapter of the ten prints a count of how far he has got, and no chapter prints a count of how far the three who went down the four-hundred-mile road on day 391 have got, and he is not going after them and is not asking about them.**
- **The woman of about sixty-nine** is in Chapter 0445 and in no other of the ten. She came up nine steps with a tin in both hands, nobody asked her what she carried it for, she waited for the room to go quiet and then asked her third question to the room and not to anybody, and **nobody in that room answered it, and about four of the nine had an answer and about four said nothing, and she went back down the steps with the tin in both hands and nobody went after her and nobody said anything to her on the way down.** The tin was empty and had been empty on the last two occasions on which she carried it up those steps.
- **Nobody else moved.** **The woman of about fifty-four is in none of these ten chapters and the descriptors `woman of about fifty-four` and `blue thread` both stand at zero across all ten files, and she is not going to be asked about anything by anybody, and the reason is not that she would not answer.** The man of about forty-one in a very good coat is in none of them and the phrase for the distance of his office stands at zero. **The woman of about twenty-one is on a road and was not asked about anything, and the days since the fourth of the four said no are not counted at any value in any of these ten.** Nobody got stronger, nothing was resolved, the third of the four places is empty and the third column is empty and one of the four marks came back in a place it had not gone out in and no chapter of these ten says one word about that.

## 2. THE STATE OF THE PEOPLE AFTER FIFTY DAYS

- **Adrian Vale** is named in **NINE** of the fifty chapters — 0402, 0405, 0414, 0422, 0428, 0432, 0436, 0441 and 0447 — and in no chapter in any form in the other forty-one, and the nine are the plan's own list and not a batch's doing. In eight of the nine he is in a room reading a page. **Chapter 0436 is the ninth and it is the one morning on which he reads nothing: a Wednesday, the hundred and twenty-first day of the Bare Month, about the seventh hour, on the boards of a salt wharf at the bottom of this city, and nobody asks him anything, nobody declines to ask him anything, nobody is rude to him and nobody in the chapter is waiting for him to be useful, and the chapter does not say he was right.** He is in 0441 and 0447 as well and in both of them he says nothing at all and is thanked for nothing. **He is Stage 2 on all fifty days, performs no working, opens no threshold, is offered no workway, is aged nowhere, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across the fifty files. HE GETS NO STRONGER AND NOBODY DOES.**
- **The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register** is the volume's decision. She reads the sheet out on day 405; she decides on day 425, in a room, out loud, with the cost named first by her and in her own words, to leave the third of the four places empty, and is thanked by nobody and told she is right by nobody; she is asked nothing and answers nothing on day 428 about what she asked; she enters the second line in the register on day 443 with a day in it and nothing under it and reads the day back before she writes anything else, which is a thing she does; she says on day 450 that the third of the four places is empty on purpose and that nobody in that room is going to put anything in it. **She is not Tamsin Quill and the two of them were in one room only in Chapter 0436, on different ends of it, and nothing in that chapter settles whether they are one woman and nothing may.** Her second slate is named in seventeen of the fifty and is picked up, turned over and written on in none of them.
- **The man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall** is asked what a line is on day 428 and answers, and the answer is the name of this volume, and it is in no file. He refuses the wall's rule on day 438 in about four seconds, and what it costs him is his own ninth line. He is thanked in none of the chapters he speaks in and asked in advance, in a lane a long way back, not to be thanked. **His chalk is still in the inside breast pocket and was not taken out on any of the fifty days, and the ninth line is not named in any chapter as the same line as the four on the sheet and no chapter has him say he knows what he said on day 428.** He is at the back of the bench on the last day with his hand flat on the pocket and says nothing.
- **The man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board** is right about a column in about four seconds on day 438 and the chapter does not say so. He is wrong about four stalls in a market on day 444, is told so in front of about nine people, does not argue and does not concede, and says out loud that he would rather have been wrong about that than right about a page. He asks the practical question about the cart on day 441, and he has his slate on his knee on day 450 and says nothing at all that morning. **He is thirty-nine and is not thirty-seven, and a word-bounded count cannot separate two men who are both a man of about thirty-eight and the decision about which occurrence is the same person is at `outline/volume-09.md` §16.13 and is not reopened.**
- **The man of about fifty-seven** is named in about twenty-three of the fifty and **is asked nothing in any of them, is not told where the fence is, does not move it and does not touch the rope.** On day 449 the length of new rope fell off the back of his chair because a woman moved the chair to let somebody past her, and he picked it up and put it back, and nobody in that room said one word about it and nobody has ever thanked him for anything. **The length of new rope has still never been used and the coil in the handcart is a different rope and the chapter that names both of them joins them in nothing. He is right about nothing in this volume and is not defeated in any of it.**
- **The man of about fifty-four's woman, a woman of about fifty-four, is Volume 09's one new person.** On a page once, in Chapter 0420, put there by being asked in a room in daylight with a date on the door where her line is. **Her second handle is a length of blue thread tied round her left wrist, which is how she has always known a length by, and it stands at two occurrences in one file across the fifty and in no other.** Her line is the one that came back moved and she is not on a page after Chapter 0420 and nobody is going to ask her about it, and the reason is not that she would not answer.
- **The man of about fifty-nine** went up a shut road out of this city on day 440 and on day 448 with a hurdle of hazel rods and a bar he replaced, stopped on the low side of the fence for about as long as it takes to turn a bar over, picked the barrow up again and went on down, and **no chapter of the fifty prints a count of how far he has got and no chapter prints a count of how far the three who went down the four-hundred-mile road have got.** He is not going after the three and is not asking about them.
- **The man of about sixty** packed about nine yards of cart-track on the low side of that shut road and then on the high side, and was not doing anything to the fence, and did not offer the man of about fifty-nine the end of a wedge, and was answered once.
- **The man of about twenty-seven** washes the store's board every morning and has decided not to wash the wall above it, and the board has stood unwritten since day 225 and its figures are the figures of day 224, and it is the only instrument this book has said has not lied to anybody.
- **The man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf** is in seven of the fifty with a tally-board and about four hundred and forty on it, and **the figure he has in his head is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume and no chapter puts it and the figure he sends in one sentence as two numbers.** The other man of about thirty-eight, who came up a lane with a barrow of boards and took nine boards off a wall on day 347, is not in this volume and the two are never merged.
- **The woman of about sixty-nine** came up nine steps three times in this series with a tin in both hands and asked a question each time, on day 346, on day 396 and on day 445, and got nothing back three times. **The tin is empty and it was empty on the last two occasions on which she carried it up those steps, and nobody in that room asked her what she carried it for and nobody has ever asked her.**
- **The woman of about twenty-one with the pen with the nib ground narrow** is on a road and was not asked about anything, **and the days since the fourth of the four said no were not counted at any value in any of the fifty. A party of four is four.**
- **Tamsin Quill** is in one chapter of the fifty, on the boards of the wharf, with a folded sheet of road with about nine places on it in the inside of her coat, and she did not take it out. The two women of twenty-nine are never in one room as subjects of the same thing.
- **Jonah Pike** is not in this volume and his absence is not a departure and is not a resolution and no chapter may use it. **The Regent is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated and not exposed and not converted. The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard. The girl of seventeen is not fetched and being unfindable is not a qualification. The boards under the chapel are not lifted, the island is not owned and the box is shut.**

## 3. AND THE FINDING ABOUT THE PROTAGONIST, WHICH IS A FINDING ABOUT A RULE AND NOT ABOUT A CHAPTER

**Seven chapters of fifty in Volume 08. Nine chapters of fifty in Volume 09. In nine chapters of fifty, in the last batch of the last volume, he is furniture in both. The rule that produced the seven is Volume 06's close finding, that he is named in the chapters in which he is reading a page and in no chapter in which people decline to ask him, and the Volume 08 close found the rule to be a rule and not a law and set a number for the next volume, and this volume raised it by one and gave him a morning in which there is nothing to read, and that is a real answer and it is also the only answer the rule can produce, because the rule selects for reading a page and a volume with no notices in it has eight rooms in which there is a page. THE RULE IS NOW TWO VOLUMES OLD AND IT HAS COST THIS SERIES ITS PROTAGONIST ACROSS TWO VOLUMES. THE REMEDY IS A DECISION AND NOT A REPAIR, no chapter of Volume 09 may be opened or moved to make room for one, the number of chapters he is in in Volume 10 is that outline's to set and this close does not set it, and the finding belongs in the brief of whatever plans the next one. A man who is only ever on a page is a piece of furniture, and the volume that worked that out is the volume that should say so out loud.**

## VOLUME 10 — OUTLINE, and this block wins over every block above it

**This is a plan's cast layer and not a record of pages. No chapter of Volume 10 exists. Every person below is proposed and is subject to a chapter saying so in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, and to the instrument rule at the end of this block, which is the most important line in it.**

### 1. THE HEADLINE OF THIS BLOCK, and it is a measurement and not a plan: THE POOL OF DESCRIPTORS A NEW PERSON MAY BE DRAWN FROM IN THIS MANUSCRIPT IS EMPTY, AND THE FIGURE THIS BLOCK CARRIED AS A NEW PERSON LAST VOLUME ALREADY HAD A PAGE

**The instrument is the published one and it validates itself: twenty-nine distinct descriptors on Volume 07's fifty files, nineteen on Volume 08's fifty, fifteen on Volume 09's fifty, and fifty-seven at zero in BOTH Volume 08's fifty and Volume 09's fifty. That last figure is the close's raw set and it reproduces exactly.**

**Re-measured on all 450 chapter files instead of on the last two volumes, and then checked against the whole manuscript: every one of the thirty-seven survivors of the close's subtraction is standing on a page in a finished volume. Thirty-seven of thirty-seven. The clean pool is ZERO. A descriptor at zero in two consecutive volumes of this manuscript is not unused; it is between uses, and a two-volume window is the instrument's blind spot and not a filter.**

**AND THE ONE THAT MATTERS IS NAMED HERE BECAUSE A MEASUREMENT NOBODY ACTS ON IS A RUMOUR. A WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FOUR was published by the last volume's plan and close as that volume's ONE NEW PERSON, measured at three occurrences in one file, taken from a set of descriptors at zero in the last two volumes, put on a page in Chapter 0420 by being asked where her line is, with a length of blue thread tied round her wrist invented on that page. SHE STANDS AT SIX OCCURRENCES IN TWO FILES ACROSS FOUR HUNDRED AND FIFTY CHAPTERS, AND THREE OF THEM ARE IN `chapters/volume-06/chapter-0294.md` AT LINES 15, 25 AND 33.** At line 15 she has had a sheet face up on a sill for four days and the chapter says of her that she is not on any roll and that this is the fourth time that week a sheet has been left face up somewhere by a woman who is not on one. At line 25 she puts the sheet down on the step, goes back in and shuts the door. At line 33 she stands on the step in the one place on that lane where the sun is not, does not call anybody, does not look up the lane, and says two sentences in her own mouth. **She was not new. NOTHING ON ANY PAGE HAS EVER JOINED HER TO THE WOMAN OF CHAPTER 0420 AND NOTHING ON ANY PAGE EVER WILL, BECAUSE A DESCRIPTOR MAP COUNTS OCCURRENCES AND DOES NOT DECIDE WHICH OCCURRENCE IS THE SAME PERSON. This phase does not repair it, does not attribute it, and does not print a number against a person across two finished volumes: settling whether these are one woman or two is a Volume 09 review repair pass and not an outline's, and the repair is owed by nobody after it.** What is decided and binds: **the descriptor is not available to anybody, no chapter of Volume 10 gives it to anybody, and the standing rule against merging two people of one descriptor is now a rule with a case on it.**

**THE SIX THAT ARE PLAINLY PEOPLE AND WERE IN THE POOL: woman of about fifty-three 4 in 1 (`chapters/volume-06/chapter-0289.md`, a woman who put a date in chalk on a door and did not put a name under it and said the day was hers and was not going to be on a list); man of about forty-two 30 in 17; woman of about fifty-nine 29 in 13; woman of about sixty-one 27 in 16; woman of about seventy 15 in 9; man of about twenty-six 17 in 5; woman of about fifty-six 17 in 14; woman of about thirty-seven 10 in 3. The whole of the remaining residue is a passing mention in a finished volume and not a scene — a girl who looked at somebody's boots, a man who has copied other men's sentences for nineteen years, a man in a fever-house, a man in a good coat who was counting, a man who believes a gate is his because he opens it, a man with eight graves and no name stone, a woman who cannot read, a woman who came down a lane with a boy, a woman with a hinge-knife, a woman with a sieve, a man who asked what number of days.**

**THE RULE THAT REPLACES IT AND BINDS EVERY PHASE AFTER THIS LINE: A DESCRIPTOR IS AVAILABLE TO A NEW PERSON ONLY IF IT STANDS AT THREE OCCURRENCES OR FEWER ACROSS ALL FOUR HUNDRED AND FIFTY CHAPTER FILES, EVERY ONE OF THOSE OCCURRENCES IS A PASSING MENTION AND NOT A SCENE, AND IT IS AT ZERO IN EACH OF THE LAST THREE VOLUMES' FIFTY. THE CROWDED FOUR ARE NOT AVAILABLE TO ANYBODY: woman of about thirty-four, man of about thirty-four, man of about forty-four, man of about thirty-nine. THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN IS NOT AVAILABLE TO ANYBODY, AND THE MAN WHO TRADES ON THE BOARD IS THIRTY-NINE. A NEW PERSON IS NOT DESCRIBED AS A MAN OR A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY, AND NO CHAPTER PRINTS A BARE TENS WORD WITHOUT A UNITS WORD. THE CHILD DESCRIPTORS — *girl of about seven*, *girl of about fifteen*, *boy of about nine*, *boy of about ten*, *boy of about fifteen* — ARE NOT AVAILABLE EITHER. *ABOUT TWO HUNDRED ROOMS* IS NOT AVAILABLE TO A NEW PERSON AND THE ATTRIBUTION STAYS UNPAID, AND NO CHAPTER PRINTS A ROOM COUNT FOR ANYBODY, IN NARRATION OR IN A MOUTH, ON A LIST OF CARDINALS OR ON A LIST THAT INCLUDES *DOZEN*.**

### 2. THE ONE PERSON VOLUME 10 PUTS ON A PAGE, AND SHE IS A RETURNING PERSON WITH NO NUMBER ON HER

**THE WOMAN WHO WALKED A MARKET. No age. No descriptor. No number.** She is on a page once already, in Chapter 0441, where the descriptor map cannot reach her and a string search for a name could never have found her, which is the whole of why she is available and it is an accident of how the last volume's chapter was written and this phase is taking the accident and calling it the only move left. She is put on a page properly on **day 453**, in a market, in weather, by being asked something she can answer, in a room in daylight with a date on the door. **Her handles are a slate under her arm and a tin in her two hands, both of which are on a page already, and the age-and-trade form is not abolished and does not apply to her because she never had one.** She answers correctly and she goes on working the morning and nobody says she is good at it. **No chapter of Volume 10 asks her why she stopped, guesses at it, improves on it or thanks her for it, and no chapter gives her a number, and a card that does any of those things is a card that has invented a person.** On **day 498** she asks the volume's new question to one man and not to the room, and he does not answer it and the room does not hear it.

### 3. The rest of the returning cast, and what each one is not

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THE STORE'S BOARD.** A bucket and a rag, a board, and a wall he has decided not to wash. He has washed it every morning since the seventh week of this flood and it has not been written on since the two hundred and twenty-fifth day. **He speaks the name of this volume on day 486 and refuses this volume's one rule on day 490. He is not thanked for either, he has asked in advance not to be, and he is not. He is the only person in this manuscript who is asked a question about something he has done every morning for two hundred and seventy-five days, and the reason the answer costs him the next day is that the rule tells his market that the figures he has kept clean were not checked by anybody either and that the reason they have stood up is not him.** This is the volume's central person and he is not new and has never been asked anything.
- **Adrian Vale.** Stage 2 on all fifty days. **Fifteen chapters: 0453, 0454, 0458, 0461, 0464, 0466, 0471, 0476, 0479, 0482, 0485, 0488, 0491, 0494 and 0496, and no chapter in any other form in the other thirty-five. In no chapter in which he is reading a page. A card may not put him in a chapter unless the card names the thing his hands are on. In 0461, 0479, 0488 and 0494 nobody asks him anything, nobody is waiting for him to be useful, and he reads nothing, and a chapter may let him work something out and must then not say he was right.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the Narrow Mark is not opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, and he is aged nowhere.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN.** A length of new rope he has never used, over the back of his chair, which fell off it on day 449 and on day 456. Right about most of what he says. Standing in front of a thing he cannot move and has not tried. **Not asked, not told, not defeated, not leaving, not happy. The rope is not used in this volume and no chapter has him cut it or take it up.** Whether the fence stands between the right two things is not settled and is not to be settled by a chapter.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD.** A board. He took a name off it in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and has never said so. **He is thirty-nine and not thirty-seven. He is wrong about four stalls in a market, told so, and not told he was right; and he is right in Chapter 0485 about a public figure in about four seconds, at a cost to himself, and is thanked by nobody and argued with by nobody and the chapter does not say he was right. He asks the volume's new question on day 498 and does not answer it.** Not defeated, not converted, does not apologise, does not take his own board down.
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER.** A second slate on a shelf behind her, a third column she leaves empty on purpose, a folded sheet of road with the fourth crease gone soft. **She reads the one-place sheet out and says what it is for before anybody asks; she decides the midpoint on day 475 and names the cost first; she asks the question that produces the volume's name on day 486; and on day 493 she is the person who opens the book she keeps and puts it back, and nobody else in that room touches the shelf.** She is not Tamsin Quill and the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room.
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE.** A tin, empty. She asked this series' question three times and got nothing three times, and **she does not ask a fourth in this volume, and she comes up the nine steps on day 459 with the tin in her two hands and nobody remarks on it.** Not asked about anything, not thanked for three questions, not improved on.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF.** A tally-board, a knife and a barrow, and about four hundred and forty. **The figure he sends is not the figure he has, and the figure he has is on no page and is printed nowhere in this volume, and a chapter may not print the two as two numbers.** He answered on day 357 and refused on day 393 and nothing in this volume may put those two acts in a room together, and he is not the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards on day 347.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL.** A piece of chalk he does not use, in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since day 298. He is right in Chapter 0454 about a page in about nine words and is not thanked and has asked in advance not to be. **He is not the man who is asked what a line is on day 428, and a card may not merge those two acts of the last volume into one man.** The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT.** A document inside the coat and no local language. He is the reason the office is nine hundred miles away and is not defeated, and the nine sentences that are his about a return are not said again.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING.** A satchel with a flap. **He carried five notices in a coat, a return in a satchel, the last volume's sheet and this volume's sheet, and he has opened none of them, and the fourth is the last of them in this flood because there is not going to be an eighth sheet that goes past a man.**
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS, TAMSIIN QUILL.** All returning, all in `outline/volume-10.md` §7.3, and **Tamsin Quill is not Tamsin Quill the register-keeper, the consent fracture is not mended, no apology is made or refused or forgiven, and the fifth condition is not given.**

### 4. The instrument rule that binds every name and every face above, and it is the last line of this block because it is the one a later phase will break

**NO NAME AND NO PLACE ARRIVES BY NARRATION IN THIS BOOK. IT IS CHECKED FOR A ROOM, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR, BY A PERSON. EVERY CARD NAMES THE DESCRIPTOR OF EVERY PERSON IN IT, AND A CARD THAT CANNOT NAME ONE IS A CARD THAT HAS INVENTED A PERSON, AND NO CARD MAY ATTACH A NUMBER TO ANYBODY WHO DOES NOT ALREADY HAVE ONE.**

**AND THE TWO FAULTS THIS LAYER HAS NOW COMMITTED TWICE, named so that a later phase inherits the reason and not only the rule: a descriptor map counts occurrences and does not decide which occurrence is the same person, which is how two men of about thirty-eight nearly became one man in Volume 07 and how a woman of about fifty-four nearly became a new person in Volume 09; and a measurement taken on a window is a fact about the window, which is how thirty-seven people came to be described as a pool of unused descriptors.**

**AND THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS PHASE WROTE MAY PRINT: the name of Volume 09 and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; the volume's one rule said on day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words also stand at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; and the day-154 sentence. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED. A PLAN IS NOT A PAGE AND A PERMANENT WITNESS SECTION IS NOT A PAGE, AND THE HOLD IS ON THIS PHASE'S OWN FILES.**

**This block is a record of pages. Ten chapters of Volume 10 exist and every line below was read off them. It adds nobody, takes nobody off a page, and gives no one a number.**

### 1. THE HEADLINE OF THIS BLOCK, and it is a measurement and not a plan: THE POOL IS STILL EMPTY, THE TEN FILES ADDED NOBODY, AND THE ONE PERSON THE BATCH PUT ON A PAGE PROPERLY IS A PERSON WITH NO NUMBER ON HER

**The instrument is the published one and it validates itself.** Run on the ten chapter files of this batch, it returns **ten distinct descriptors, and every one of the ten is a returning person: *man of about twenty-seven* 11 in 4 files, *man of about thirty-four* 11 in 5, *woman of about twenty-nine* 10 in 7, *man of about thirty-nine* 7 in 4, *man of about thirty-one* 4 in 2, *woman of about sixty-nine* 4 in 1, *man of about thirty-eight* 2 in 1, *man of about fifty-seven* 2 in 1, *man of about forty-one* 1 in 1, *man of about sixty* 1 in 1.** Volume 09's fifty files returned fifteen and Volume 08's returned nineteen and Volume 07's returned twenty-nine, and **ten is a consequence of a batch in which no chapter adds a person and not a decision anybody took.**

**AND THE ROW THAT IS THE FINDING: the woman who walked a market is at ZERO on that map, and she is in four of these ten chapters, and she is the only person this batch put on a page properly.** She has no age, no descriptor, no number and no name on any page, and **the descriptor map cannot reach her and a string search for a name could never have found her, and that is the whole of why she was available.** **The volume's cast decision was executed on day 453 and it held: she was asked something she can answer, in daylight, where she was already standing, by a person; she answered correctly, in the order, without stopping in the middle of it; she went back to work; nobody said she was good at it; nobody asked her where she learned it or how long she had been coming round that market; and nobody asked her why she stopped.** A card that attaches a number to her has invented a person.

**NO DESCRIPTOR WAS ADDED TO ANYBODY IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND NO AGE WAS ADDED TO ANYBODY. The pool at `outline/volume-10.md` §19.5 is still empty, still measured on all four hundred and fifty files, and this batch took nothing from it. The crowded four are untouched. The man of about thirty-seven is not available to anybody and is not in these ten chapters. *Woman of about fifty-four* is not available to anybody and is not in these ten chapters and is still two people until a repair pass says otherwise.**

### 2. What each person did in these ten days, and what each of them is not

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THE STORE'S BOARD.** A bucket and a rag, a board, and a wall he has decided not to wash. **He is in four of the ten chapters and he is the only person in the batch who appears in every kind of place the batch uses.** On day 451 he takes the board down, washes it and puts it back up with the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day of this flood on it and nothing else on it, and he says four sentences out loud about the wall above it, and nobody agrees with him and nobody disagrees with him out loud. On day 453 and day 460 his board goes up its wall in the background of a market morning and nobody looks at it. **On day 458 he puts the rag into Adrian Vale's hands without a word, goes back inside his store, and does not teach him and does not stop him and does not say one word about it; he comes out afterwards with a broom, sweeps past the trough, says nothing, and goes back in; and the two of them put the board up on its two nails together without exchanging a word.** He is asked nothing in any of the ten chapters. **He speaks the name of this volume on day 486 and refuses this volume's one rule on day 490, he is thanked for neither, he has asked in advance not to be, and he is not — and no chapter of these ten touches either act or connects them.**
- **THE WOMAN WHO WALKED A MARKET ON TWO FEET.** A slate under her arm with nothing written on it, and a tin in her two hands, and both of them are on a page already. **In four chapters: 0453, 0454, 0459 and 0460.** In 0454 she is in a room and does not speak to Adrian Vale and he does not speak to her. In 0459 she says the one thing in that room that nobody else is saying, and it is about stopping, and nobody asks her why. In 0460 she holds the sheet and says nothing about it. **She asks the volume's new question on day 498 to one man and nobody answers it, and no chapter of these ten is that chapter.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN.** A length of new rope he has never used, over the back of his chair. **He is in one chapter of these ten, Chapter 0456, and he is asked nothing in it and told nothing.** He reads the figure out loud because it was in front of him and nobody takes it up. **The rope comes off the back of his chair at about the eleventh hour, he picks it up and puts it back, and nobody in that room says one word about it, and the room's not-noticing is the chapter.** He says a true thing about the half he paid money for and nobody in that room has ever asked him about any of it. **Not defeated, not converted, does not leave, is not happy. The rope is not cut and not taken up and not used. Whether the fence stands between the right two things is not settled and these ten chapters do not settle it.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL.** A piece of chalk he does not use, in an inside breast pocket, not taken out since the forty-third day of the Short Month, which is day two hundred and ninety-eight of this flood, **printed at full length in Chapter 0454 and shortened to a relative phrase in none of the ten.** He is in five chapters. **In Chapter 0454 he says the thing that is wrong with a page in twelve words — that nobody can tell from a page how long it has been lying there — and nobody improves on it, nobody thanks him, and he has asked in advance, a long way back, in a lane, not to be thanked.** In 0460 he turns a page over, looks at the fold, says nothing about it, and is not thanked. **He is not the man who is asked what a line is on day 428, and no chapter of these ten mentions that act or mentions the ninth line except to say that nobody in that room connected the two things. The ninth line is not moved, dated, read aloud, taken down, thanked for or resolved.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD.** A board. He is in seven of the ten chapters. **He is right about a page in Chapter 0454 and in Chapter 0452 and nobody tells him so either time, and he asks a question in Chapter 0452 that is the question in that room and nobody thanks him for asking it.** He took a name off his own board in his own hand in about the ninth year of this flood and has never said so. **He is thirty-nine and not thirty-seven. He is right in Chapter 0485 about a public figure and is not thanked, and that chapter is not one of these ten. He asks the volume's new question on day 498 and does not answer it.**
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER.** A second slate on a shelf behind her, a third column she leaves empty on purpose, and a folded sheet of road. **She is in five of the ten chapters and she is the one who reads the one-place sheet out on day 455 in the local language in daylight with a date in chalk on the door in front of about nine people, and who says out loud what it is for before anybody asks.** In 0452 she looks at a satchel once and does not look at it again. **The register is not opened in any of these ten chapters and the third column is empty on purpose on all ten days and the second column is empty on all ten days; the register's third line is entered on day 493 and the day on that line is day 490 and both are named in that chapter. She decides the midpoint on day 475, names the cost first, and asks the question that produces the volume's name on day 486. She is not Tamsin Quill and the two women of twenty-nine are never in one room.**
- **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE.** A tin, empty. **She is in one chapter of these ten, Chapter 0459, and she comes up the nine steps with the empty tin in her two hands and she does not ask a fourth question and nobody in that room remarks on it.** She has asked a question in that building three times, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, and each time it was a different thing and each time she was answered by nobody. **Nobody remarked on the not-remarking, and the reason is an instrument and not a decision about her, and the chapter says the reason once in narration and does not say it in a mouth.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF.** A tally-board, a knife with a blunt edge, and a barrow. **He is in one chapter of these ten, Chapter 0457, and he is right in about four seconds about what the sheet is a count of and is thanked by nobody and argued with by nobody and the chapter does not say he was right.** He sends about four hundred and forty in the return on the quarterly round. **The figure he has is a second set of notches on the tally-board in his coat, is not a figure, is on no page, and is printed nowhere in this volume and appears in no mouth in these ten chapters as a figure.** He answered on day 357 and refused on day 393 and **nothing in Chapter 0457 puts those two acts in a room together, he does not say he knows what he said, and neither speech is repeated.** He is not the man who came up a lane with a barrow of boards.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT.** A document inside the coat and no local language. **He is in one chapter of these ten, Chapter 0455, and he says one thing, badly, in the local language, and he does not describe that room to anybody.** The nine sentences about a return that are his are not said again and are not paraphrased and are not improved on.
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING.** A satchel with a flap. **He is in one chapter of these ten, Chapter 0452, he puts it on a table and keeps his hand flat on the flap for a moment and then takes his hand off it, he is not thanked for carrying it, and he does not open it.** He has carried five notices in a coat, a return in a satchel, the last volume's sheet and this volume's sheet, and he has opened none of them, and the fourth is the last of them in this flood.
- **ADRIAN VALE.** Stage 2. **He is in three of these ten chapters and in no other, he is aged nowhere, he reads nothing in any of the three, and he is asked nothing in any of the three.** In 0453 his hands are on a barrow of stall boards and he is spoken to by nobody. In 0454 his hands are on the satchel going round the room and he does not speak to the woman who walked a market and she does not speak to him. **In 0458 he is handed the rag and he says seven words — that there is a number under the water — and nobody takes it up and nobody asks him a second question, and the chapter does not say he was right about anything he worked out and does not say what the figures of the two hundred and twenty-fourth day are a count of.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the other world is not named once, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across all ten files.
- **TAMSIIN QUILL, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS.** All returning, all in `outline/volume-10.md` §7.3. **The man of about sixty is named once in these ten chapters and he is in narration and not on the road.** Tamsin Quill is not in these ten chapters and the consent fracture is not mended, no apology is made or refused or forgiven, and the fifth condition is not given.

### 3. The instrument rule that binds every name and every face above, and it is the last line of this block

**NO NAME AND NO PLACE ARRIVED BY NARRATION IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS. EVERY CARD NAMED THE DESCRIPTOR OF EVERY PERSON IN IT AND EVERY CHAPTER MATCHED ITS CARD'S CAST, and the batch added no person and took no descriptor from anybody. THE POOL IS EMPTY MEASURED ON ALL FOUR HUNDRED AND FIFTY FILES, AND THE ONE PERSON THIS BATCH PUT ON A PAGE PROPERLY IS A PERSON WHO IS ALREADY ON ONE AND WHO HAS NO NUMBER, NO AGE AND NO DESCRIPTOR, AND SHE WAS PUT THERE BY BEING ASKED IN DAYLIGHT BY A PERSON.**

**AND THE THING A LATER PHASE WILL BREAK IF IT IS NOT CARRIED, named here because the ten days produced the case that made it necessary a second time in one volume: A CARD CAN NAME THE WRONG PERSON. Card 2 and Card 5 of this batch's own card file put a prohibition on the man of about thirty-nine on the ground that he was asked what the column is for on day 357, and the man asked on day 357 is the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf. The pages were right and the cards were wrong, and the pages were right because the standing rule against merging two men of one descriptor was already written down. A descriptor map counts occurrences and does not decide which occurrence is the same person, and a card file is a map with a cast attached to it.**

**AND THE ONE THING NO FILE THIS BATCH WROTE MAY PRINT: the name of Volume 09 and the words the man of about thirty-four says in Chapter 0428; the volume's one rule, said on day 438; the answer spoken in Chapter 0357; the refusal spoken in Chapter 0393, whose six words also stand at `bible/power-system.md:1684` and at `outline/volume-08.md:208`; and the day-154 sentence. NAMED WHERE EACH OF THEM IS, PRINTED NONE OF THEM, NO ABSENCE CERTIFIED. THE HOLD IS ON THIS BATCH'S OWN FILES AND NOT ON WHERE A STRING STANDS.**
