# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0005 — Chapters 0541 to 0550, days 541 to 550

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO BLOCK ABOVE IS EDITED. The full record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-11-batch-0005.md`.**

**NO CHARACTER MOVED OUT OF ITS PLACE, NO DESCRIPTOR CHANGED, NO NEW PERSON ENTERED THIS VOLUME, AND NOT ONE THING WAS RESOLVED THAT WAS NOT ALREADY THE PLAN'S TO GIVE.**

1. **ADRIAN VALE.** **In three of these ten days — 0543, 0546 and 0549 — and in seven he is not named at all.** The wants are the plan's and not this batch's: a knot in a string round the near corner of a form that is not the knot he was shown, and is the same different one when he does it again; flags dry by the fourth hour, which they were not; and four figures written out where anybody at that end of a market could see the order of them, which he did not do. **He has no quoted word in any of the three, he reads no page in any of them, he is aged nowhere, nobody speaks to him, nobody thanks him, nobody tells him he was right, nobody in those chapters is waiting for him to be useful and he is not useful to one person in them.** The three things that happen to somebody else because of him are a cart lifted over a bucket, a knot worked at with a thumbnail, and a board-foot put on a blank slate. None of them is a decision, a figure, a document, a notice or a panel. Still Stage 2; no working performed; no threshold opened; the other world not named; the words *passage* and *privilege* at zero across the ten.
2. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER.** In six of these ten days, and opened the register on three of them and shut it again inside the hour on all three, and no hand but hers went near that shelf on any of the ten. **On day 541 she is the one who paid the first cost and she says nothing whatever about it while another woman names it out loud. On day 545 she put the sheet into the satchel with her own two hands and did not say one word about it. On day 548 she asked this volume's new question of one man and not of a room, and afterwards said out loud that she is not going to say it better than it was said the last time it was said in that room.** She is thanked by nobody on any of the ten days. Her third column is empty on all ten and nobody asked her about it.
3. **THE WOMAN WHO MADE THE FIGURE.** **She is on no page of days 541 to 550 as a person and she is named in one of them as a slate under an arm and a tin in two hands, which is the handle the plan gives her and not a descriptor.** She has no name, no age, no number and no descriptor on any page of this manuscript and none was given her here. **She is not in any of the ten rooms. Nobody in any of them can say her name, one man stands up in the middle of the room on day 547 and does not ask who made the figure and sits down again, and nobody has asked her anything about why she stopped. Her figure went out of this city on day 545 in a satchel with a flap and it is not withdrawn and it is not coming back and no chapter of these ten sends anybody after it.**
4. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING.** In four of these ten days. **On day 545 he carried the form out of this city and did not open it and nobody asked him to and nobody in this city is going to, and he said out loud that he has carried five notices and a return and two sheets without opening one of them and that he is not going to be the one who opens this one.** On day 543 he had to work at a knot in a loop of string across his own strap with the flat of his thumb nail and said nothing while he did it. Nobody thanked him on any of the ten days and nobody improved on him.
5. **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD.** **On day 542 he washed the wall above the top of that board, from the top down, and stopped with the rag in his hand about two fingers above the figures and put the rag on the stone lip without touching them, and that is the only day of the fifty on which that wall is washed.** Nobody asked him why and nobody thanked him and no second person touched that wall and no mouth in any of the ten days says why. On the other nine days of this batch he washed his board and stopped where his washing has always stopped, and nobody asked him anything.
6. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL.** In six of these ten days and asked nothing in any of them. **His own piece of chalk has not come out of his inside breast pocket on any of them and the full form of the day it went in is printed at full length in Chapter 0549 and is not shortened to a relative phrase; the store's chalk is in its box on the step with the lid standing open and is used in no chapter of these ten.** He said two things on day 549 about the two chalks and nobody asked him about either. He was not thanked and nobody told him he was right.
7. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD.** In six of these ten days. **On day 544 he said out loud, twice, that nobody in that room has asked him what was in the space about two fingers wide on the face of his board for nine years, and nobody asked him. On day 550 he said out loud that a wall of figures is read across and a book of figures is read down and that there is nothing in four hundred miles that will turn one of them round and stand it beside the other one, and nobody asked him which one a person should be using.** The name that is not on his board is not printed in any of these ten chapters.
8. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-NINE WHO WALKS THE FOUR-HUNDRED-MILE ROAD.** **On page for the first time in this volume, on day 548, and it is the day this volume's new question is put to him.** He has walked that road four times in nine years, he has not gone down it since three people went down it, he is not asking about them and nobody in that room asked him about them. **He did not answer the question and the room did not hear it.** He put the hurdle he made against the wall by that door, stood at that window, and at the eleventh hour took his bar from under his arm and went down that stair and out along the flags and up the road at the near gate, and nobody asked him what the bar was for or where he was going.
9. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WITH THE HANDCART.** In one of these ten days, and **a handcart had to be lifted over a bucket standing in the middle of a row and nobody in that row knew whose hands put it there and nobody asked him anything.** The coil of rope and the hazel mallet with its head bound in cord were not used on that day and nobody asked him about the fence.
10. **THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN.** In one of these ten days, with a length of new rope over the back of his chair, and **no hand in that room lifted it, nobody in that room said one word about it, and nobody in that room asked that man anything at all.**
11. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE.** **On no page of days 541 to 550. Her three questions are unanswered on all ten of them and no fourth was asked.**
12. **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, Tamsin Quill.** All off the page for these ten days. **The figure the man of about thirty-eight has is still on no page and the figure he sends is printed nowhere in this batch, and he is in none of these ten chapters.**
13. **AND NO DESCRIPTOR WAS TAKEN AND NO POOL WAS DRAWN FROM: the descriptor map on these ten files is eight distinct descriptors and fifty-three occurrences and not one of the eight is new, and the whole-manuscript figure is seventy-eight on five hundred and forty-nine files and seventy-eight on the five hundred and thirty-nine that were on disk before this batch.**

# VOLUME 11, BATCH 0005 — REVIEW REPAIR PASS

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO BLOCK ABOVE IS EDITED. The full account is `state/batch-summaries/volume-11-batch-0005.md` §7.**

**NO CHARACTER MOVED OUT OF ITS PLACE IN THE STORY, NO DESCRIPTOR CHANGED, NO NEW PERSON ENTERED THIS VOLUME, AND NOT ONE THING WAS RESOLVED THAT WAS NOT ALREADY THE PLAN'S TO GIVE. WHAT CHANGED IS WHERE ONE WOMAN IS STANDING AND NOTHING ELSE.**

1. **ADRIAN VALE.** **Still in three of these ten days — 0543, 0546 and 0549 — and in seven he is not named at all.** He still has no quoted word in any of the three, reads no page, is aged nowhere, is spoken to by nobody, is not thanked, is not told he was right, is not waited on to be useful and is not useful to one person in them. **The three things that happen to somebody else because of him are still a cart lifted over a bucket, a knot worked at with a thumbnail, and a board-foot put on a blank slate, and none of them is a decision, a figure of this city, a document, a notice or a panel.** Still Stage 2; no working performed; no threshold opened; the other world not named. **Two recuts touched his two room chapters and neither touched a want, a want or a fact: in 0543 the sheet is still shut on that table and the figure in its one place has not been outside this city, and in 0546 he is on the flags after the man of about twenty-seven rather than before him, which is the want `outline/volume-11.md` §4.1 publishes for that chapter and which the page now states.**
2. **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER.** Still in six of these ten days, and still opened the register on three of them and shut it again inside the hour on all three, and no hand but hers went near that shelf on any of the ten. **The one recut that touched her is in 0547, and it changed what a sentence denies and not what she does: the second slate is still not touched, she is still the only person who handles anything on that shelf, and she still went to it once.**
3. **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD.** **Unmoved.** He still washed the wall above the board on day 542 and on no other day of the fifty, still from the top down, still stopped about two fingers above the figures, and still put the rag on the stone lip without touching the board or the figures. **Two later chapters that described that wall in the colour it had before he washed it now describe it in the colour his washing left, and that is the only change to him and it moves nothing he did.**
4. **THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY WITH THE HANDCART.** **Unmoved, and his position in Chapter 0546 is now stated once instead of twice.** He still came up that row at the sixth hour, still had to lift the front of that cart over a bucket standing in the middle of it, still went on with one wheel off the ground, still came back down at the seventh hour and still stopped at the near gate with the shafts down in the wet and the mallet in the cart and the rope over the side of it. **The closing paragraph no longer leaves him two rows up a row, and nobody in that row knows whose hands put the bucket there and nobody asked and nobody is going to.**
5. **THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE'S COST-NAMER, WHICH IS A WOMAN AND HAS NO DESCRIPTOR OF HER OWN.** **She now stands at the middle of that bench in Chapter 0547, where the first edition gave her no place at all, and she is still not the person the cost is about, and she is still thanked by nobody and improved on by nobody.** The card placed her near the step and the card is left standing and the difference is published.
6. **THE WOMAN WHO MADE THE FIGURE.** **Still on no page of these ten days, and her figure still went into the one place on day 538 and out of this city on day 545 without her on it.** This pass did not put her on a page, did not name her and did not give her a descriptor, and the one recut in her chapter removed a phrase that had come from an earlier volume and said instead that there is no place in that room where a name like hers could be written down.
7. **THE MEN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE, THIRTY-FOUR, THIRTY-NINE, FIFTY-SEVEN AND FIFTY-NINE.** **All unmoved, and all still thanked by nobody.** The man of about thirty-one still carries the satchel and still never opened it and still worked at a knot with the flat of a thumb nail. The man of about thirty-four still keeps his own piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket and it did not come out. The man of about thirty-nine still has nineteen rates on a board with a space about two fingers wide and nothing in it, and still said in Chapter 0544 that nobody has asked him in nine years. The man of about fifty-seven still has a length of new rope over the back of his chair that no hand lifted. The man of about fifty-nine still stands at a window and did not answer the question.

**AND NO NEW PERSON WAS INTRODUCED, NO DESCRIPTOR WAS TAKEN, AND THE DESCRIPTOR TABLE STANDS AT EIGHT AND NOT ONE OF THE EIGHT IS NEW. THE THREE THINGS THIS PASS CHANGED IN THE TEXT ARE A COUNT OF DAYS THAT WAS WRONG THREE TIMES, A WALL'S COLOUR IN TWO CHAPTERS, AND WHERE ONE UNDESCRIBED WOMAN IS STANDING.**

# VOLUME 11 CLOSE

**This block is at the foot of the file and it wins over every block above it. NO ENTRY ABOVE IS EDITED, and in particular line 223 is not touched. The full record is `state/volume-11-close.md`.**

**WHO IS WHO ON DAY 550, COUNTED AND NOT DESCRIBED, and the descriptor counts are this close's own on Volume 11's fifty files: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall in 32 chapters at 53 occurrences, the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board in 24 at 45, the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register in 28 at 45, the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living in 15 at 27, the man of about twenty-seven who washes the store's board in 14 at 27, the man of about sixty at a corner with a hazel mallet and a coil of rope over his shoulder in 7 at 11, the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf in 5 at 6, the man of about fifty-nine who walks the four-hundred-mile road in 1 at 5, the man of about fifty-seven in 4 at 4, and the woman of about fifty-two in 1 at 2. TEN DESCRIPTORS, TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-FIVE OCCURRENCES, NONE OF THEM NEW, AND THE TENTH IS THE ONE THE CLOSE'S OWN FIRST EDITION OF THIS TABLE LEFT OUT.**

**AND THE ONE PERSON THIS VOLUME PUT ON A PAGE PROPERLY IS STILL A PERSON WITH NO NAME, NO AGE, NO NUMBER AND NO DESCRIPTOR ON ANY PAGE.** She has a slate under an arm and a tin in her two hands and that is her whole handle, and she is on a page in nine of the fifty chapters, and she is the subject of four mornings of physical work and of a discovery, and nobody in this flood has asked her why she stopped, and her figure went out of this city without her on it. **NO PHASE THAT IS NOT ENTITLED MAY GIVE HER A NAME, AN AGE, A NUMBER OR A DESCRIPTOR, AND NOBODY MAY ASK HER WHY SHE STOPPED, AND A CARD THAT GIVES HER A SPEECH EXPLAINING IT IS A CARD THAT HAS TURNED HER INTO A DOCUMENT.**

**AND THE STATE OF THE TWO MEN AT THE ENDS OF THAT BENCH ON DAY 550, in the state the volume left them and no state file may improve on. The man of about thirty-four asked the man of about thirty-nine one question on day 534, the first either had ever asked the other, and asked in advance not to be thanked and is not; his own board stands face in against his own trestle with the bare back of it out in the row and his piece of chalk is in his own inside breast pocket and is not coming out of it. The man of about thirty-nine answered it once, out loud, in the local language, and was thanked by nobody, and nobody in this city has ever asked him what he calls a thing he has never said, and the space about two fingers wide on the face of his board is still not filled.**

**AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING REFUSED THIS VOLUME'S ONE RULE ON DAY 540 IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AT THE COST OF HIS OWN SILENCE, and nobody thanked him, nobody argued with him and he did not ask to be, and the cost is the whole of what he has, which is that he has carried five notices, a return, a sheet on day 401 and a sheet on day 451 in this flood without opening one of them, and this is the fifth thing he has carried that he knows what is in.**

**AND THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD IS IN THIRTEEN OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS AND IS ASKED ABOUT NOTHING IN ANY OF THEM, and he washed the wall above that board once, on day 542, and nobody asked him why and nobody thanked him, and the same washing of the same three sets of figures is on the board on day 550 that was on it on day 501.**

**AND THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN IS IN FOUR OF THE FIFTY CHAPTERS WITH A LENGTH OF NEW ROPE OVER THE BACK OF A CHAIR THAT NO HAND LIFTED AND NOBODY ASKED HIM ABOUT, and the woman of about sixty-nine is in one chapter having not asked a fourth question, and the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf is in five with a tally-board nobody picked up, and the man of about thirty-eight who came up a lane with a barrow of boards is in no chapter of this volume and is not merged with anybody.**


## VOLUME 12 OUTLINE — the three cast placements, the eleven protagonist chapters, and the things that were not done to anybody

**This block was written by the Volume 12 outline phase. It invented no person, took no descriptor, and drew nobody by narration. The clean pool for a new person is ZERO on five hundred and forty-nine chapter files, measured by a reading of thirty-three lines and not by a sweep, and the arithmetic returns twenty candidates and every one of the twenty is attached to somebody with a page or a scene in a finished volume, and four of the twenty are barred outright by a rule about the form of a descriptor — two bare tens words and two children. The plan is `outline/volume-12.md` §7 and §19.6 and the twenty are at §19.6 with the file and line of every one of the thirty-three lines they stand on.**

### The three returning people this volume moved from the edge of a room to the middle of a task

- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT A SALT WHARF — the decider, and the person the volume's figure is about.** A tally-board, a knife, a barrow, and a figure of this city on the side the sun does not get to, lying face down on a stack of boards. **He turns it face up on day 577 and it cannot be put back, and on day 575 he decides, in a room with a date in chalk on the door, in daylight, before about nine people, that the figure on it is the figure that goes in the one place under the heading on the sheet that came up the road, and that a line of words goes under that figure, and that no line of words goes under any other figure in this city. HE SAYS WHAT IT IS FIRST, IN ABOUT ELEVEN WORDS, IN HIS OWN MOUTH, AND THE COST IS HIS, AND HE IS THANKED BY NOBODY. HE IS IN THE ROOM AND HE IS NOT ON THE PAGE HIS OWN FIGURE GOES OUT ON, AND NOBODY IN THIS VOLUME ASKS HIM WHERE THE FIGURE CAME FROM AND HE DOES NOT SAY. HE IS GIVEN NO NEW DESCRIPTOR AND NO NEW AGE AND NO NEW NAME, and the figure is printed nowhere in this volume and the figure he sends is printed nowhere either. HE IS NOT THE MAN WHO CAME UP A LANE WITH A BARROW OF BOARDS ON DAY 347, and nothing on a page has ever put the two in a room.**
- **THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — the refuser, and the man who is right about a page and is thanked for it three volumes running.** A board standing face in against his own trestle with the bare back of it out in the row, and a piece of chalk in his own inside breast pocket that has not been out since day 298 and is not out in this volume either. **On day 590 he refuses this volume's one rule in about four seconds, and the cost of the refusal is that he has agreed with a page out loud, in front of about nine people, which he has never done in this flood, and the room heard him and nobody thanked him and nobody argued with him and he did not ask to be. - **THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER — the reader of the page and the speaker of the name.** Her second slate on the shelf behind her with a day across the head of it and nothing whatever under that date, which is never picked up, never turned over and never written on, and no hand but hers near that shelf on any of the fifty days. **She reads the third one-place form out on day 555 and says what it is for before anybody asks, and on day 578 the man of about thirty-nine asks her what she calls the thing her own second slate is for and she says a thing out loud once, in four to eight words, in the local language, and it is the name of this volume and it is in no file including the plan, and this block does not print it, does not paraphrase it and does not say where it stands. **In 0557 his hands are on the lid and the grit and nothing else, and he wanted the lid to shut, and it shut, and the only reason it shut is that he carried the grit off the flags in front of it and nobody asked him to and nobody noticed. She said one sentence out loud about somebody having put the water off her front, she said she did not know who and was not going to stand there and work it out, and NOBODY IN THAT ROW ANSWERED HER, nobody thanked him, nobody told him he was right, nobody in that chapter was waiting for him to be useful, and he was not useful to anybody in it. THE MAN WHOSE OWN BOARD STANDS FACE IN TWO STALLS ALONG DID NOT KNOW IT HAPPENED AND THIS BATCH DID NOT TELL HIM.**

**NOTHING ABOUT ANY PERSON IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS CHANGED, AND THAT IS THE LINE THAT MATTERS MOST IN THIS BLOCK.** No one got stronger, no one got weaker, no one was thanked and no one thanked anybody, and no cast member gained a name, an age, a number or a descriptor. A repair pass reworded five of the ten files and lengthened a sixth, and **it did not move a single person, because the findings were about sentences that repeated each other, not about who said them.** The chapter each of them is in, counted across days 581 to 590 and verified against the ten files: the man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall in EIGHT of them; the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf in FIVE; the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register in SEVEN; the man of about twenty-seven who washes that board in TWO; the man of about thirty-nine who trades on a board in ONE; the man of about thirty-one who carries things for a living in ONE; and Adrian Vale in TWO, 0583 and 0586, and in no other.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL.** He said the thing that is said in 0588 and in 0589, and he said other things in 0581, 0582, 0583, 0584, 0587 and 0590, and nobody thanked him on any of the eight. **On 0590 he agreed with a page out loud, in about four seconds, in front of about nine people, and that is still the only time he has done it in this flood.** The sentence in 0584 that carried his saying the thing that is said to that sheet was reworded; **he still said it, and it still took him about four seconds, and nobody in that room improved on it, and nobody asked him a second question about it.** His piece of chalk is still in his own inside breast pocket and did not come out on any of the ten days.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF.** He is in 0583, 0584, 0588, 0589 and 0590 and was asked nothing by anybody in this city on any of them. **He read the line of words out once, on 0589, in about nine words, in his own mouth, and he named the cost of it out loud after that, and nobody thanked him for either, and he is not on the page the figure went out on, and being in that room did not help him on 575 and did not help him on 589.** The chapter he read it out in is one paragraph longer than the version first published, and **the paragraph that was added is about the line being in about nine heads and in no book, and it is not about him and it does not thank him.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER.** She wrote the figure in the one place and the line of words under it, on two different days and in two different hands, and she read neither back to anybody. She folded the sheet on 0590 with the bone, put the flat of her own hand on it, and took her hand off it. **The nine-word sentence in 0581 in which her room did not say she was right has been reworded; the room still did not say she was right, and it still did not improve on it, and it still did not thank her for saying it, and it still did not ask her a second question about it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD.** He is in 0582 and in 0587, and on 0587 he washed it twice from the same end, and the two runs of water down the stone and the four of them one under another are now on the page, and the bucket with the rag folded over its rim and the four people who looked at the wet stone and not at that board are now on the page too. **He was not thanked on 0587, nobody has ever asked him anything, and the wall above the top edge of that board was still not washed by him and still not by a second person.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD.** He is in 0581 and in no other of the ten, and one of the two sentences reworded in that file stands next to him. **He still brought the board up that stair and still stood it against the front of that bench, and the space at the foot of the column between the other two is still about two fingers wide with nothing whatever in it, and nobody in that room asked him what was in it and nobody put anything in it.**

**ADRIAN VALE.** He is in 0583 and 0586 and in no other of the ten. Nothing about him changed and nothing about his two days changed, and the sentence that was reworded in 0581 is about the room and not about him.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING** is in 0590 and in no other of the ten, and he is not in that room, and the line that said he would not be in it again now names the round instead of an interval. **He still has not opened anything he has carried, and this batch did not spend that, and the day he carries it is not one of these ten.**

**THE PERSON WITH A SLATE UNDER AN ARM AND A TIN IN HER TWO HANDS.** Named once, in 0585, as the person who walked eleven miles of flats. **No repair pass gave her a name, an age, a number or a descriptor, put her in a room, thanked her, or asked her why she stopped, and nothing about her changed.**

## VOLUME 12, BATCH 0005 — CHAPTERS 0591 TO 0600. NO NEW PERSON, NO NEW DESCRIPTOR, NO NEW NAME.

**THE WHOLE OF WHAT CHANGED ABOUT ANYBODY IN THESE TEN DAYS IS IN FIVE LINES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A HANDLE BESIDE THE AGE AND THE TRADE AND NEVER INSTEAD OF IT.**

**ADRIAN VALE.** He is in 0592, 0596 and 0599 and in no other chapter of these ten, and the day map's column is the authority and no card moved him. In 0592 his hands were on the near edge of a tally-board, on his own thumb and on a length of cord at a salt wharf, and what he wanted the thing for was for that figure to be one somebody in this city is able to check, and he did not get it, and what he did there was to leave a line of grey grit across a number. In 0596 his hands were on a rag, on a step and on the box of chalk with its lid, and what he wanted the thing for was for that board to be washed once and not twice, and he got it, and what it caused was a man two stalls along going away with a mark on his own hand. In 0599 his hands were on a shelf, on the spine of a register and on the ledge under a second slate, and what he wanted the thing for was for somebody in that room to be asked what the column on the right of that page is for, and nobody was asked, and a man said the thing out loud instead and the room did not do it. **None of the three is a lesson and none of the three is a reward, and in none of the three is anybody waiting for him to be useful, and in all three he is the cause of at least one thing that happens to somebody else, and nobody thanks him and nobody tells him he was right.** He is Stage 2 on all ten days, he performs no working, no threshold is opened, the other world is not named once across these ten files, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, he is aged nowhere, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across all five hundred and ninety-nine chapter files.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF — a tally-board, a knife, a barrow, and a figure of this city's own on the side of it that the sun gets to now.** He is in 0591, 0592, 0593, 0594, 0595, 0598 and 0599 and he is asked nothing on any of the seven. He has a line of grey grit across that figure that he has not wiped and is not going to wipe, and nobody in this city has ever been shown the working of that figure. His own figure went out of this city on the round on the five hundred and ninety-fifth day of this flood with a line of words under it, and he is not on it, and he was in the room when it was written. **He is the person the volume's figure is about and he was in the room and being in the room did not help, and Chapter 0594 is the chapter in which nobody asked him where his figure came from and the chapter does not say why.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — a board standing face in against his own trestle, and a piece of chalk he does not use.** He is in every one of these ten chapters but one, he carried a stool from a wall to the end of a bench on 0591 because a man was standing, he kept two fingers on the near edge of another man's board on 0593 so that it would not slide, he put the flat of his own hand on a board on a wall on 0596 and walked away with grit on it, he asked the volume's question on 0598 and put it to one man and not to a room, he said the first thing anybody has said out loud about the register's right-hand column on 0599, and his piece of chalk is in his inside breast pocket at the end of 0600 and it has not been out of that pocket since the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood. **He refused the wall's one rule out loud in about four seconds on day 590, which is not one of these ten, and nobody thanked him for it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD — a board under his arm, and nineteen rates, and one name that is not on it.** He is in 0591, 0593, 0594, 0598, 0599 and 0600. He has a space about two fingers wide at the foot of the column between the other two. Nobody in this volume asked him what was in it and nobody in this volume asked him to put anything in it. He was asked one question on 0593 and did not say where the space came from. **He was put the volume's new question on 0598 and did not answer it, and the nine people in that room did not hear it and the chapter says so in the next line, and no chapter of the fifty asks him what the missing name is and no chapter of the fifty joins that question to the question about a name in a column.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER — her second slate, and a right-hand column she leaves empty.** She is in 0591, 0593, 0594, 0595, 0599 and 0600. She wiped a date off the outside of a door on 0591 and chalked the day of that morning back on and put no name under it. She read the third one-place form out on 0555, wrote the line of words under a figure on 0588, and on 0595 she put that sheet into a satchel with a flap and let the flap down over it and did not do the second turn of the strap herself. **On 0599 a man said out loud in her room that a column like the one on the right of her page is where a word goes, and she did not put anything in it, and she is not thanked for that and nobody asked her why, and on 0600 she said the last thing said out loud in that room and it was that she is leaving that column empty.** The second slate is still on its shelf with a day across the head of it and nothing under it and it was not picked up, not turned over and not written on on any day of this volume.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING — a satchel with a flap, and one thing he knows what is in.** He is in 0592, 0595 and 0597. **On 0595 he took a sheet with a figure of this city's own in its one place and a line of words under that figure out of a room over a market and down a stair, and nobody in that room asked him what was in it and he did not open it, and on 0597 he carried it through standing water at a near gate and up a stretch of wet road and did not open it there either.** He says in about nine words in 0597 that there is nobody in this city he could tell what is in it and there was never going to be. He was not thanked, he was not argued with, and he is not on any page of the volume.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD — a bucket and a rag, and a wall he washed once and has not washed since.** He is in 0596, 0597 and 0600. On 0596 he found that the board had been washed once and not twice and he did not ask anybody which of the two was right. On 0600 he washed it in two passes as usual and the wall above its top edge was not washed. **Nobody has ever asked him anything in this volume.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT WHO CAME UP A LANE WITH A BARROW OF BOARDS is a different man from the man of about thirty-eight at the salt wharf, and 0592 is the chapter that says so on the page, and nothing in this volume has ever put the two of them in a room.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY — a hazel mallet with its head bound in cord and a coil of rope in a handcart.** He is in 0597 at a near gate, he looked at the standing water and at the man going through it, and he took the cart back the way he had come. **He put the fence up out past the last named house in this flood, he was not sent, and nobody has asked him about it since and nobody is going to.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN is on no page of these ten. His length of new rope is named twice, in 0593 and in 0600, as a length of new rope over the back of a chair, unused, unlifted, uncut and untaken up, and he was not asked one thing about it by anybody in this city in this flood.**

**THE PERSON WITH A SLATE UNDER AN ARM AND A TIN IN HER TWO HANDS IS IN NONE OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS AND WAS GIVEN NO NAME, NO AGE, NO NUMBER AND NO DESCRIPTOR BY THIS BATCH. She is not in a room, she is not thanked, and nobody asked her why she stopped.**

**AND THE FIGURES ARE AT §6, §8 AND §9 OF `state/batch-summaries/volume-12-batch-0005.md` AND ARE NOT RESTATED HERE.**

**THE NEXT PHASE IS `workspace/volume-12/close/PROMPT.md` AND IT IS A VOLUME CLOSE AND NOT A BATCH.**

## VOLUME 12 CLOSE — CHAPTERS 0551 TO 0600, DAYS 551 TO 600. NO NEW PERSON, NO NEW DESCRIPTOR, NO NEW NAME.

**THE WHOLE OF WHAT CHANGED ABOUT ANYBODY IN FIFTY DAYS IS IN TWELVE LINES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A HANDLE BESIDE THE AGE AND THE TRADE AND NEVER INSTEAD OF IT.** The descriptor instrument, published and validated on Volume 09's fifty files before use, returns **NINE distinct descriptors in TWO HUNDRED AND TWENTY-TWO occurrences** across the fifty files of this volume, and every one of the nine is a descriptor this manuscript already had on a page in a finished volume. The pool of clean candidates for a new person is **ZERO, by the reading and not by the arithmetic, and this volume took none of the twenty candidates the arithmetic returns, drew no person by narration, and invented nothing, for the third volume running.** One row of the instrument is a fault of form and is published: **FIVE bare tens descriptors, every one of them the man of about sixty, three in Chapter 0562 and two in Chapter 0597, and the plan's own cast row and the plan's own prose rule are in tension on that one descriptor and the tension is resolved by nobody.**

**ADRIAN VALE.** Stage 2 on all fifty days, in eleven of them and in no other, and the day map's column is the authority and no close may edit it. **HIS HANDS ARE ON SOMETHING IN ALL ELEVEN AND HE IS THE CAUSE OF AT LEAST ONE THING THAT HAPPENS TO SOMEBODY ELSE IN ALL ELEVEN, and nobody thanks him and nobody tells him he was right and nobody in any of the eleven is waiting for him to be useful and he is not useful to them.** Five of the eleven wants were obtained and six were not, and neither set is a reward and neither is a lesson: in 0551 the second pass of the wash was the last thing he did before the light came off the flags; in 0557 the lid of a box shut because he carried the grit off the flags in front of it; in 0577 the fold was the fold the last sheet of that shape went out with, twice and a half with a bone; in 0586 the second row was crossed by something a person could walk on and a woman of about twenty-nine walked across it and does not know whose hands laid it there; in 0596 that board was washed once and not twice and a man two stalls along went away with a mark on his own hand. He did not get it in 0561, 0565, 0571, 0583, 0592 or 0599. **The volume behind this one published at the end of its own fifty that not one of its eleven was obtained; this one obtained five and did not obtain six, and the difference is a fact about what the days were and not about him.** He performs no working, no threshold is opened, the other world is not named once across the fifty, nobody offers him a workway and he asks for none, he is aged nowhere, and the words *passage* and *privilege* are at zero across all five hundred and ninety-nine chapter files.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-EIGHT AT THE SALT WHARF — a tally-board, a knife, a barrow, and a figure of this city's own on the side of it that the sun gets to now.** He is the decider of this volume and the person its figure is about, and he named the price out loud first and the reason for the price is his, and there was no vote and the four days that would have been the cooling are not available in this city. **He turned his own tally-board face up on the five hundred and seventy-seventh day with one hand on its near edge and nobody in that yard said a word about it. His figure went out of this city on the round on the five hundred and ninety-fifth day with a line of words under it, and he is not on it, and he was in the room when it was written.** He is asked nothing on any of the fifty days. **Chapter 0594 is the chapter in which nobody asked him where his own figure came from, he did not say, and the chapter says in narration that nobody has ever found out why and does not say why.** He is not the man of about thirty-eight who came up a lane with a barrow of boards, and **Chapter 0592 says so in narration on the page**, which settles in prose what a descriptor is not supposed to settle, and that is published at `state/volume-12-close.md` §8 item 5 and repaired by nobody.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL — a board standing face in against his own trestle, and a piece of chalk he does not use.** He refused the wall's one rule out loud on day 590 in about four seconds, in front of about nine people, **and the cost of the refusal is the whole of what he has: he has said what is wrong with a page in about nine words, three volumes running, and been thanked by nobody three times, and agreeing with a page out loud in a room is the one thing he has never done in this flood.** His chalk is still in his inside breast pocket at the end of day 600 and has not been out since the two hundred and ninety-eighth day of this flood. He carried a stool from a wall to the end of a bench on day 591 because a man was standing. He kept two fingers on the near edge of another man's board on day 593 so that it would not slide, and then asked where the space at the foot of the column between the other two goes, and got an answer that it came from nowhere he was going to say. He put the flat of his own hand on a board on a wall on day 596 and walked away with grit on it. He asked the volume's question on day 598, to one man and not to a room, and it was not answered. **He said the first thing anybody has said out loud about the register's column on the right in this flood on day 599, and the room did not do it, and he is not thanked for it and is not argued with.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-NINE WHO TRADES ON A BOARD — a board under an arm, and nineteen rates, and one name that is not on it.** He asked the question that produced the volume's name on day 578 and got an answer that went nowhere and that he has not repeated to anybody and did not write down. He was put the volume's new question on day 598 and did not answer it, and the nine people in that room did not hear it, and the chapter says so in the next line. He has a space about two fingers wide at the foot of the column between the other two and **nobody in any of the fifty asks him what was in it and nobody in any of the fifty asks him what the missing name is and nobody in any of the fifty joins the volume's question about a name in a column to the space on his own board.**

**THE WOMAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-NINE WHO KEEPS A PUBLIC REGISTER — her second slate, and a right-hand column she leaves empty.** She read the third one-place form out on day 555 and said what it was for before anybody asked. She named the first cost out loud on day 583, in about eleven words, and the person it fell hardest on was standing at the near end of that bench and heard all of it. She copied a figure out of a book into her own hand on a clean sheet on day 584 and said out loud that she did not make it. She wrote the line of words under a figure on day 588, and said on day 595 that she wrote those words and did not count that figure, and put the sheet into a satchel with a flap and let the flap down over it and did not do the second turn of the strap herself. **She looked at the column on the right of that page four times on day 574 and said nothing about it four times, and on day 599 she said out loud that she is leaving it empty, and on day 600 she said the last thing said out loud in that room, which was that five figures in that book this morning and five yesterday and nobody has ever asked that room for a sixth.** The second slate is on its shelf with a day across the head of it and nothing under it and it was not picked up, not turned over and not written on on any day of this volume.

**THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-ONE WHO CARRIES THINGS FOR A LIVING — a satchel with a flap, and one thing he knows what is in.** He carried the third one-place form up the road on day 552 and did not open it, and the same form, written in and shut, down that road on day 595, and he did not open it then either, and he carried it through standing water at a near gate and up a stretch of wet road on day 597 and did not open it there. He says in about nine words on day 597 that there is nobody in this city he could tell what is in it and there was never going to be. **He was not thanked, he was not argued with, and he is not on any page of this volume.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT TWENTY-SEVEN WHO WASHES THAT BOARD — a bucket and a rag, and a wall he washed once and has not washed since.** He washed that board at the fourth hour on ten of the fifty days. He found one pass on it where there have been two on day 596 and did not ask anybody which of the two was the right one. He said on day 587 that it had been washed and it was going back up and there was nothing in that row that morning that anybody had to decide. **Nobody has ever asked him anything in this volume.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY — a hazel mallet with its head bound in cord and a coil of rope in a handcart.** He put the fence up out past the last named house in this flood, he was not sent, and on day 597 he stood at a near gate and looked at standing water and at a man going through it and took the handcart back the way he had come. **Nobody in this city has ever asked him a question about that fence, and his descriptor is a bare tens word that the plan's own prose rule bars and its own cast row uses, and that tension is published at `state/volume-12-close.md` §7.6 and resolved by nobody.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-SEVEN is on one page of the fifty, on day 566, standing at a corner of a fence. Nobody puts a question to him, nobody asks him where the fence is or by whom or when, he answers one statement in about nine words, and the chapter says in its own words that he is not going to be asked anything about that fence in this city by anybody. His length of new rope is named on six pages of the fifty and is not used, not cut, not taken up and not lifted on any of them, and no hand in the fifty lifts it.**

**THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-THREE, THE MAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-FOUR, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FOUR, THE WOMAN OF ABOUT SIXTY-NINE, THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-ONE IN A VERY GOOD COAT AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-SEVEN are on no page of this volume's fifty, and the woman of about sixty-nine does not ask a fourth question, for the fifth volume running, and the man of about thirty-seven is not available to anybody.**

**THE WOMAN WHO WALKED A MARKET — a slate under an arm and a tin in her two hands, and NO NAME, NO AGE, NO NUMBER AND NO DESCRIPTOR ON ANY PAGE.** She is on a page of this volume, on eleven miles of flats on day 585, and she is in no room, and she is given none of the four, and nobody thanked her and nobody asked her why she stopped, and the tin was not opened at either end of that walk and no figure came off that ground on that day and there is nowhere in this city to put a fifth one.

**TAMSIN QUILL is on no page of this volume's fifty.** **AND THE FIGURES ARE AT `state/volume-12-close.md` §7 AND ARE NOT RESTATED HERE.**
